  **For Whopper (detent), in plain words.** When `Status` has `needs_confirmation` (or the `NeedsConfirmation` signal arrives non-empty) show, for a dataset of the person's files: "`<discard>` of `<held>` files were removed on the server. Remove them here too?" with two buttons, "Remove" and "Keep". Remove calls `ConfirmDiscard(dataset)`; the files go on the next sync, which starts at once, and the message disappears when the signal arrives empty. Keep calls nothing: the files stay here, the message stays, and syncd checks again on every cycle (at its slowest every six hours); if the server shows the files again the message goes away by itself, and if it does not the message stays (and comes back after a restart), so Keep means "not now", not "never ask". Suggested help under Keep: "Keep the files here. You will be asked again if they are still gone." If `discard` equals `held`, say "All `<held>` files were removed on the server." A `NothingHeld` answer means someone else already decided: just refresh. Keep the words plain: say "files" and "online" if "server" is too technical, never "dataset" or "sync cycle".
- **rel-6c (the shell never heard a held deletion; fix-notice).** Verified: `Core::relay` sent an event only to rostered connections the hub `admits`, and `Access::admits` lets `SheetHost` see only datasets its app name owns; a connection joins the roster only by calling syncd. So sill, which owns no dataset and had no reason to call syncd, was told nothing. Changes: (1) `Access::hears_holds` = `admits` or role `SheetHost`; `Hub::hears(caller, event)` uses it for `Event::Held` only (Progress and Conflict still need `admits`), `Hub::holds_for(caller)` lists the holds on now. `admits`, `visible`, `Status`, `Pause`, `Resume`, `Resolve` and `ConfirmDiscard` are unchanged, so the shell is refused all of them on a dataset it does not own (`NoFittingAccount`); Settings keeps confirming. (2) New additive method `Sync1.Watch()` (no arguments, no result; porter-dbus `SyncProxy::watch`, skeleton, `dbus/org.quire.Sync1.xml`, introspection counts methods 6 to 7). `Status` could not serve: it takes a `<account>/<dataset>` name that must parse and be visible, so an empty or "any" name is `InvalidArgs`. Frozen-interface rule as in rel-6b: an added method, no existing member, argument or error changes, nothing in porter-core's wire types, so no `VocabVersion` bump. Any identified caller may call it (unknown sender: `AccessDenied`); it joins the roster and sends the caller, as unicast `NeedsConfirmation` signals before the reply, the holds on now that its role hears (the shell: all; an owning app: its own; a stranger: none). (3) `NeedsConfirmation` details gain `account` (`s`), the account's object path (`/org/quire/Accounts1/account/<segment>`), when held (additive; the ended signal stays empty). The dataset name `<account>/<dataset>` already carried only the lossy object-path segment of the account id (`object_segment`), not the id and not the app; the path is what Settings can open. (4) `driver.rs`: the status is published before `Event::Held` is told (one line), so a `Watch` that lands between the signal and the publish still finds the hold. Consumer impact: `syncd::service::held_details` takes the dataset name first (grep of origin/master consumers, see the report); `SyncProxy` gains `watch`. Tests: `service::hub_tests::the_shell_hears_every_hold_and_nothing_else_of_a_dataset_it_does_not_own`, `service::status::tests::the_hold_signal_carries_the_counts_and_the_account_while_held_and_nothing_when_it_ends` (unit); `tests/it/confirm_bus.rs` (jailed gate) `the_shell_hears_a_hold_and_its_end_but_no_data_of_a_dataset_it_does_not_own` (a shell gets the hold with `account` and its end, no Progress or Conflict while the owner gets Progress, Status/Pause/ConfirmDiscard refused and the hold still on), `a_shell_that_joins_after_the_hold_started_is_told_at_once` (and a stranger that joins is told nothing); `porter-dbus` `syncd_and_inferd_declare_what_they_did`. Each fails without the fix: the old relay never selects a `SheetHost` for a dataset it does not own, and `watch` did not exist.
  **For Whopper (sill), at start.** Call `org.quire.Sync1.Watch()` once on the `org.quire.Sync1` name (`/org/quire/Sync1`, via `porter_dbus::SyncProxy::watch`) after subscribing to `NeedsConfirmation`, and again whenever the `org.quire.Sync1` name gets a new owner (syncd restarted: the roster is in its memory). If a hold is already on, the signals arrive before `Watch` returns. The signal is `NeedsConfirmation(dataset s, held a{sv})`. Held: `{discard: t, held: t, account: s}` where `account` is the account's object path, `/org/quire/Accounts1/account/<segment>`; open Settings at that account with it. Ended: an empty dict (the `dataset` argument says which one ended; clear the notification you posted for it). Post one notification per `dataset` string and replace it on a repeat. Suggested words: title "Files removed online in `<account name>`" (the name from the account's own display name via Accounts1), body "`<discard>` of `<held>` files were removed online. Open Settings to decide." (or "All `<held>` files were removed online." when equal); activating it opens Settings at that account, where detent shows Remove and Keep (rel-6b). sill must not call `ConfirmDiscard` itself (it is refused for the shell).

## Lane fix-flows (user journeys, audit ux-1..ux-9, rel-11, ship-5; base `7861a79`, 2026-10-09)

- **ux-2 (verified: `MicrosoftEnv::system()`/`GoogleEnv::system()` read the clients files once, when accountd built its families; a client id typed in Settings was unused until accountd restarted).** `porter_families::ClientFiles { shipped, own }` (additive): an environment made `with_files` (or `with_client_files`, or `system()`) reads both files again at every sign-in, token refresh and revoke (`MicrosoftEnv::clients()`/`GoogleEnv::clients()`; a handed-in `registry` is used as before when no files are set, which is what every test rig does). accountd builds its OAuth families from `Paths::client_files()`, so the file the families read is the one Settings writes (`FamilyIo::clients`, `FamilyIo::system(providers, clients)`). Cost: one small file read per sign-in and per token renewal. Tests: `env_common::tests::the_files_are_read_again_each_time_so_a_row_written_later_is_seen`, `tests/it` `microsoft::signin::a_client_id_written_after_start_is_used_by_the_next_sign_in` (needs a client id, the row is written, the same provider's next sign-in opens the browser with that id; before the fix the registry was fixed when the provider was made), accountd `paths::tests::the_families_read_the_clients_file_settings_writes`.
- **ux-1 (part) / ux-3 hide (verified: `add_flow::drive` listed every signable provider, so Microsoft with no shipped client and Google with none at all were rows that could only end `NeedsClientId`).** `porter_provider::Readiness { Ready, NeedsClient }` and `Provider::readiness()` (a default method answering `Ready`: every other family, the fakes and mailo's providers are unchanged). The Microsoft and Google families answer `NeedsClient` while `clients().lookup(issuer, channel)` finds nothing (the files are read at that moment, ux-2), `FamilyProvider` forwards it, and the add list (`add_flow::listed`) leaves such a provider out each time a sheet opens; a hint naming it is `Unavailable`, as for any unlisted provider. Signing an existing account in again is not affected (the sign-in still says it needs a client id). Not here: the shipped Microsoft client itself (ship-2, the owner's Entra registration). Tests: porter-service `tests/it` `add_flow::a_provider_with_no_client_is_not_listed_until_one_is_set` (left out, hint refused, then listed at the next sheet once the provider is ready; before the fix the first list had it), porter-families `google::env::tests::google_is_ready_only_with_a_client_of_its_own_channel`, and the ux-2 Microsoft test checks `readiness` before and after the row is written.
- **ux-4 (verified: agent rows were listed whatever the roster said; picking one ended `NoLauncher` after the sheet showed `Working`, and no launcher ships for any agent yet).** When the host keeps a roster (accountd always does: `set_launcher_roster` reads its live registrations), the add list leaves out an `agent_login` provider whose program no launcher has registered (`add_flow::launcherless`), asked at each sheet, so the row is there at the next sheet once a launcher registers (no restart). A request naming such an agent answers `NoLauncher` before any sheet opens. The check at `Start` stays for a launcher that leaves between the list and the pick. A host with no roster (an app hosting porter in process) lists agents as before. Tests: porter-service `tests/it` `add_flow::an_agent_with_no_launcher_is_not_listed_until_one_registers` (empty list, `NoLauncher` by name, then listed and added once the roster has it; before the fix the first list had the row), `add_flow::adding_an_agent_whose_launcher_left_ends_no_launcher_and_stores_nothing` (the old no-launcher test, now with a roster that has the launcher when the list is drawn and not at the pick).
- **ux-3 Settings rows (verified: `settings_keys::ISSUERS` was `[Microsoft]`; the writer rebuilt the file from `ClientsFile`, which would have dropped a Google row's `testing`/`byo` lines).** `ISSUERS = [Microsoft, Google]` and `SECRET_ISSUERS = [Google]`: Settings has `accounts.clients.google` ("Google sign-in key": "Google accounts can be added once you paste a sign-in key here: make one for a desktop app in your own Google Cloud project, and paste its secret below. Leave it empty to remove it.") and `accounts.clients.google.secret` ("Google sign-in secret": "The secret Google shows beside the sign-in key. Google asks for it even from an app on your computer; it is not your password."), both `Advanced`, both text. New `accountd::client_rows::edited`: the writer changes only the row's `client_id` or `client_secret` and keeps its other lines and the other rows; clearing the key removes the row (and its secret); a secret with no key yet is `BadValue` "Set the sign-in key first, then its secret."; a new Google row says `testing = true` (a person's own Google client is in testing until Google verifies it); the result must parse as a clients file. `Get` of the secret row answers the secret (it is the client's, the same for every user, not the person's credential; Settings role only). Setting the secret is not audited (the id change is, `ClientIdChanged`; a secret change would need a new event: open, small). Microsoft's row and wording are unchanged. With ux-2 and ux-1, a Google key set here shows Google on the add list at the next sheet. docs/google.md still tells the owner to write the row by hand (fix-ship's file; both ways work). Shared file: `write_client`'s write tail (temp file and rename) is unchanged, so fix-store's fsync (rel-8) merges beside it. Tests: `client_rows::tests` (four), `settings_keys::tests::google_has_a_sign_in_key_row_and_a_secret_row`, bus `settings::a_google_key_and_its_secret_set_in_settings_are_the_families_google_client` (before the fix the Google rows did not exist: `UnknownKey`).
- **ux-8 (verified: the Settings `reauth` action spawned the sign-in and only `eprintln!`ed a refusal; every account but an agent got the row, local runtimes included).** When the sign-in ends, accountd says how on the row: `Changed("accounts.<id>.reauth", <word>)` with `done` (signed in again), `cancelled` (the person closed the sheet), `refused` (the service said no), `no_launcher`, or `failed` (anything else: unreachable, no client for the issuer, the store failed); the shape is the sign-out row's (`Core::announce_row`, which `announce_sign_out` now uses too). The action's own `Set` still answers `false` at once, as before. A `local_runtime` (and an `auth = none`) account has no "Sign in again" row, and invoking its key is `NotPermitted` "this account has nothing to sign in to". For detent: show the word on the row (e.g. `failed`: "Could not sign in. Try again later."; `refused`: "The sign-in was refused."; `cancelled`: nothing, or "Not signed in."; `no_launcher`: "The assistant's app is not running."; `done`: "Signed in."). Tests: `settings_keys::tests::a_local_runtime_is_not_offered_sign_in_again`, bus `settings::a_sign_in_again_that_ends_without_signing_in_is_said_on_its_row` (a mail account whose provider is not loaded: the word is `failed`; the runtime's row is absent and refused; before the fix no word came).
- **ux-9 (verified: an `agent_login` account always got "Sign out" and never a sign-in row, whatever its state; a Settings `reauth` for an agent would have gone through the add-flow sign-in, not the launcher).** An agent account in `NeedsLogin` gets `accounts.<id>.reauth` labelled "Sign in" (action "Sign in"; help "Asks the assistant to sign itself in, which needs its app to be running.") and no "Sign out"; in any other state it gets "Sign out" only, as before. Invoking it asks the agent's launcher (`Host::login_agent` as the shell, the path an app's `Reauthenticate` of an agent takes; the sheet shows the wait) and the row says how it ended (the ux-8 words: `done`, `no_launcher`, `refused`, `cancelled`, `failed`). A `sign_out` `Set` while not signed in is still accepted (it was, and is harmless: the state stays `needs_login` and the pane hears `login_untouched` or the launcher's answer); only the row is not offered. For detent: the row set depends on the state, so read the schema again when `accounts.<id>.state` changes. Tests: `settings_keys::tests::an_assistant_is_offered_sign_in_when_it_needs_one_and_sign_out_when_signed_in`, bus `agent_launcher::settings_offers_sign_in_to_an_assistant_that_needs_one_and_it_asks_the_launcher` (the launcher gets the login request, `ready` makes the account `ok`, the row says `done`, the schema then offers "Sign out"), and `agent_login::the_state_shows_in_the_settings_schema_and_signing_out_forgets_it` now expects "Sign in" while `needs_login` and "Sign out" once `ok` (before the fix it pinned the opposite).
- **rel-11 (verified: `Core::sheet` started a Request and a sheet for every call, so an app could stack any number of sheets on the person).** `request::OpenSheets`: one open sheet per app (the `AppId`, so a second connection of the same app counts) and `SheetKind` (`Choose`, `AddAccount`, `Reauthenticate`; a launcher's `RequestAgentGrant` counts as a `Choose` for its agent's app). The claim is held by the sheet's task and freed however the sheet ends (answered, `Close`, the caller leaving, a panic). A second ask while one is open answers the bus's own error `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.LimitsExceeded` ("this app already has a sheet of this kind open") from the method itself, with no sheet and no Request object. No existing `Refusal` fits (each is about the account, the grant or the person: `Unavailable` says the account or store cannot be reached), and a new one would be a wire vocabulary change every consumer matches on, so none was added; porter-client's bus transport turns it into `TransportError::Malformed("bus: org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.LimitsExceeded: ...")` (its `bus_error` keeps only `Unreachable` and `Denied` apart); a typed `TransportError::Busy` would be an additive porter-client change, left open (ask, for whoever needs to tell it apart). Sheets Settings starts from its own module (`reauth`, the ux-8/ux-9 rows) do not go through `Core::sheet` and are not counted. Tests: `request::tests::an_app_has_one_open_sheet_of_each_kind_until_it_ends`, bus `bus_sheets::a_second_sheet_of_a_kind_while_one_is_open_is_refused_until_it_ends` (a second `Choose` from the same connection and from a second connection of the app is refused with no second sheet, another app's is not, and after `Close` the app may ask again; before the fix the second opened a second sheet). porter-client's `accountd_requests::a_token_that_is_not_a_path_segment_or_is_in_use_is_refused` asked a second `Choose` with the same token to see "in use"; it now does so with an `AddAccount` (another kind, same token: `InvalidArgs`) and checks a second `Choose` is `LimitsExceeded`. porter-client's `dbus_sheets::two_sheets_at_once_from_one_connection_each_get_their_own_answer` ran two `Choose`s at once and expected both answered; it now runs a `Choose` and an `AddAccount` at once (each its own answer) and checks that a second `Choose` while one hangs is refused with no second sheet.
- **mailo ask: AlreadyAdded reaches the app (verified: a sheet that ended `SignInFault::AlreadyAdded` answered the app `Refused(Unavailable)` through `refusal_of`).** New `AccountsReply::AlreadyAdded(AccountId)` (additive; serde `{"kind":"already_added","v":"<id>"}`), the id of the account already there (`add_store::already_added` now returns it; the same login at the same servers, or the same agent). `run_add` answers it when the sheet closes after `AlreadyAdded` (the sheet still shows the failure and the person closes it); nothing is stored or granted. A chooser's "Add Account…" (`add_and_allow_for`) still answers `Unavailable` (a `Choose` has no such answer). Bus: `AddAccount`'s `Response` code 0 carries `account`, `path` and `already_added: b true` (additive key; an older reader takes it for `Added`, which leads it to the same account). porter-client: `ClientError::AlreadyAdded(AccountId)` (additive variant) from `Accounts::add_account`, whose return type is unchanged. No VocabVersion change (the reply is not stored; VocabVersion stays 11). Consumers (grep of origin/master): no exhaustive match on `AccountsReply` or porter-client's `ClientError` in sill, detent, mailo or docket (mailo's `link/client.rs::failed` and `ui/add_account/host.rs` have catch-all arms; mailo can now match `ClientError::AlreadyAdded(id)` / `AccountsReply::AlreadyAdded(id)` and go on to `request_grant`). Tests: porter-client `end_to_end::adding_an_account_that_is_already_here_tells_the_app_which_one_it_is` (in process, over the generic DAV family and a fake DAV server: the second add is `Err(ClientError::AlreadyAdded(<first id>))` and nothing is added; before the fix it was `Refused(Unavailable)`), porter-dbus `sheet::every_answer_survives_the_response_and_keeps_its_code` (the new reply crosses the `Response`), porter-core `round_trip`, and porter-service's two duplicate tests and porter-families' `acceptance::adding_the_same_login_twice_is_refused_and_another_server_is_another_account` now expect `AlreadyAdded(<held id>)`. porter-rig's `add_account` command says `{"result": "already_added", "account": <id>}` (it said a generic failure); a desktop scenario that waited for a refusal on a second add of the same login reads this word now.
- **sec-3 follow-up: inferd's settings module by unit (verified: `dist/inferd.toml` had `settings = ["org.quire.Settings"]`, and `peers::CallerTable::rows` made each a row with no unit, so any process in an app scope named `app-org.quire.Settings-N.scope`, which any program can start, was the Settings caller of inferd's settings module).** `[callers] settings` now lists units: `settings = ["org.quire.Settings.service"]` (the unit accountd's callers.toml pins, Whopper's answer for detent), a row with that unit, app `org.quire.Settings`, role `Settings`, so `ProcCallers` gives the role only to the unit's main process (fix-trust's `MainPids`: the systemd manager's `MainPID`, or `<proc root>/units/<unit>` in a fixture), and the rows come before the apps' so an `[apps]` entry cannot claim the unit. An entry that is an app name (the old form, still in a person's own `inferd.toml`) stands for `<app>.service`, never for an app scope; `CallerTable::from_toml_text` refuses an entry that is neither, and the config loader (serde) skips one (fail closed). `CallerTable::with_settings` takes `BTreeSet<String>` (was `BTreeSet<AppName>`; no caller outside inferd). Note for Whopper: `desktop/scenarios/accounts-local-runtime.sh` writes `settings = ["org.quire.Settings"]`, which now means the unit `org.quire.Settings.service`: a scenario that runs detent in an app scope is refused by inferd's settings module, as accountd's own Settings row already does since fix-trust. Tests: `peers::tests::settings_is_a_unit_and_an_app_name_stands_for_its_service`, `peers::tests::only_the_settings_units_main_process_is_settings` (fixture tree: the unit's main process is Settings, another process in its cgroup is nobody, an app scope of the app's name is not Settings; before the fix the app scope was), `dist::the_shipped_table_names_settings_by_its_unit`, and `dist::the_sample_configuration_names_the_settings_app_and_its_example_rows_are_accepted` now expects the app scope to be an `App` and the unit to be `Settings`.
- **ship-5 (verified, the half the triage kept: iCloud, Fastmail and Yahoo are `auth = app_password` and their form asked for `FieldKind::Password` like any other, with nothing telling the person their account password will not work; the password IS tried at add since 0a0e868).** Picked the additive enum variant over a hint: `porter_core::sheet::FieldKind::AppPassword` (serde `app_password`), "an app password: one the person makes in their account's settings for this computer". The generic family's first form (`GenericSignIn::form`, through `password::password_for`) shows it in place of `Password` for a provider whose `[auth] kind = "app_password"` (iCloud, Fastmail, Yahoo; GMX and the typed-server forms keep `Password`), and reads the secret from either kind (`password::typed_password`: a host may answer with the kind it worded). accountd's terminal `add` words it "App password". No VocabVersion change (sheet views are not stored). Where to make one, for the shell to say under the field (plain words, keyed by the sheet's provider id): `icloud`: "Apple asks for an app-specific password here, not your Apple Account password. Make one at account.apple.com, under Sign-In and Security, App-Specific Passwords (two-factor authentication must be on)."; `fastmail`: "Fastmail asks for an app password here, not your account password. Make one in Fastmail under Settings, Privacy & Security, App passwords."; `yahoo`: "Yahoo asks for an app password here, not your account password. Make one in your Yahoo account under Account security, Generate app password." Consumers (grep of origin/master): **mailo does not compile against this until it adds arms**: `mail-app/src/ui/add_account/map.rs` matches `FieldKind` exhaustively twice (the `FormPart` split, where `AppPassword` is `SignIn`, and the `FieldKind`/`FieldRole` mapping, which needs a role or to map it to `Password`). sill matches it nowhere exhaustively (sill-model's `draft.rs`/`step.rs` use `matches!` and guards), so it compiles, but an older sill that does not know `app_password` fails to read the whole `SignIn` view of these three providers (serde): sill must take the variant (and its label "App password" and the help above) before this porter reaches the desktop. detent and docket: no use. Tests: porter-families `tests/it` `generic::the_providers_that_take_only_an_app_password_ask_for_one` (iCloud, Fastmail, Yahoo ask for `AppPassword`, GMX for `Password`; before the fix all three asked `Password`), `generic::a_fixed_file_signs_in_to_the_servers_it_names_and_the_password_logs_in` (now expects `[Address, AppPassword]` and answers in that field), `password::tests::the_typed_password_is_the_app_password_else_the_password`.

## Lane fix-hang (flaky gates, audit rel-13; base `7861a79`, 2026-10-09)

- **The inferd hangs (verified, reproduced, a daemon bug).** `agents::outside_the_grant_...`, `proc_root::a_caller_in_the_fake_proc_root_...`, `cloud::local_only_refuses_...` and `replayed::a_chat_session_is_answered_from_the_cassette` each hung a gate to the 600 s limit; any inferd bus test could. What waited: the test's first method call to `org.quire.Inference1` (`Open`, `Availability`, `OpenEndpoint`, `Prepare`, `Rescan`), for a reply that never came. Why: zbus starts a connection's object server lazily, at the first `object_server()`, on a task of its own, and that task only then adds the match that receives method calls; a call that reaches the connection before it listens matches no receiver and is dropped, with no reply and no error (the connection builder's `serve_at` waits for the task; `object_server().at()` does not). `service::serve_on` registered the object and claimed the name at once, so on a loaded machine the name could be owned, and the first caller's call arrive, before the dispatcher listened. The real daemon has the same window (the first call after a start, or a call D-Bus activation queued for the name). Fix: `inferd::service::dispatching(connection)`, called by `serve_on` before `request_name`, pings the connection itself (`org.freedesktop.DBus.Peer.Ping` through the bus, 50 ms per look, 30 s bound) until it is answered, so the name is claimed only once calls are taken. accountd's `serve_with` had the same order (it does more before claiming, so it lost a call more rarely: one hang in 200 stress runs, `agent_login::set_agent_state_audits_a_change_once_and_a_repeat_not_at_all`) and gets the same wait (a private copy in `core.rs`; accountd does not depend on inferd). Tests: `inferd::it start::a_call_made_as_soon_as_the_name_is_owned_is_answered` and `accountd::it start::...` (the same): the daemon connection's object server is started on a runtime the test holds back for 2 s; a daemon that claims its name first is "serving" at once and the test's call is dropped (no answer in 10 s: fails), one that waits is answered (both pass in about 2.3 s, the hold).
- **The accountd audit failure (verified, a daemon bug).** `agent_launcher::login_and_logout_requests_and_their_state_changes_are_audited_without_the_login` read the audit as soon as the launcher heard the request, but `Launchers::ask` wrote `AgentLoginAsked`/`AgentLogoutAsked` after the signal was sent, so the line could be missing, and a launcher's quick reply could put its state line before the ask. The line is now written before the signal leaves (a send that fails leaves the line and answers `NoLauncher`). Test: `agent_launcher::a_request_is_recorded_before_the_launcher_hears_it` (an audit sink that waits up to 500 ms, at the line, for a listener on the launcher's connection to hear the request: written after the signal it has heard it, written before it has not).
- **Every wait of the tests named above has a deadline (60 s, `hosting::bus::{DEADLINE, within}`)**: the private bus's address (read on a thread), each connection, and every method call on a rig connection (`method_timeout`); the World's fake accountd and `serve_on`; `proc_root`'s wait for the name (which also fails at once, with the daemon's standard error, if inferd exits) and its `Availability` (only `AccessDenied` counts as "not named"; any other end fails); `OpenEndpoint` and the agent HTTP client's connect, write and whole read; `cloud`'s `Open`, send and each event. A hang is now a failure that says what it waited for.
- **Runs.** `~/rs-wt/fix-hang/stress.sh` runs the gate's jail on the lane's archive with a filter and `--stress-count` (lane-local nextest config: a test is ended at 120 s). Before the fix, `binary_id(inferd::it) | binary_id(accountd::it)` (255 tests) 200 times: 8 inferd runs failed, every one a first call to Inference1 timing out (`members`, `probed`, `replayed` twice, `speech`, `cua`, `agents`, `voice_chat`). With the inferd fix, 200 times: no inferd failure, one accountd hang (above). With both, 300 times (257 tests): no inferd failure and no hang; one failure, `bus_sheets` (below).
- **Left (other lanes' files).** syncd's `service::object` registers its objects and claims `org.quire.Sync1` the same way (`syncd/src/service/object.rs`, before `request_name`) and needs the same wait. accountd's sheet host in its test rig (`tests/it/common/mod.rs`) and `settings` module served after the name are not covered. Also seen in the stress runs, not caused by this and not fixed: `accountd::it bus_sheets::a_caller_that_closes_the_request_takes_the_host_sheet_down` ("timed out waiting for the host to be told to close", 4 in 200) and `process_credential::a_launcher_that_leaves_ends_its_credentials_and_only_its_own` (1 in 200). inferd's settings object (`serve_settings`) is registered after the name is claimed, so a Settings call in that window is answered `UnknownObject` (an answer, not a hang).

## Lane spaces (Space ids per app but linkable, and the desktop-wide Space registry; base `260a14d`, 2026-10-09)

Owner decision (2026-10-09): Spaces are per app, but linkable. Each app keeps its own Spaces (quire's Spaces kit, `ds_style::space::list::SpaceId(u64)`); a Space may be linked to a desktop-wide Space; only linked Spaces share agent memory and consent across apps. porter owns the ids and the registry of desktop-wide Spaces; quire's kit stores only an opaque `link: Option<String>` (quire v0.2.31) and never depends on porter; docket and almanac key memory and consent by porter's `SpaceId`. sill no longer mints Space ids (space.rs said it did).

- **The id (`porter_core::space`).** `SpaceId` stays one string newtype with the same serde (`try_from`/`into` String), `parse`, `as_str` and `Display`. Its text grammar: `desktop` is outside any Space (reserved, as before); a bare slug of the id grammar (`[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]*`, at most 64 bytes) is a desktop-wide Space (`DesktopSpace`), so every id stored before (`work`) reads as linked with no migration; `app:<app name>:<n>` is app `<app name>`'s own Space `n` (`LocalSpace(u64)`, canonical decimal: no sign, no leading zero). `AppName` is reverse DNS with `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` elements and the slug grammar has no `:`, so the app form can never be a slug, and splitting at the last `:` is unambiguous. `SpaceId::parse` accepts all three; `kind() -> SpaceKind { Outside, Linked(DesktopSpace), App { app, local } }`; `SpaceId::linked`, `SpaceId::app`, and the helper every app calls, `SpaceId::for_app(&AppName, LocalSpace, Option<&str>) -> Result<SpaceId, CoreError>`: a link gives the linked id (a link that is not a desktop-wide id, `desktop` included, is an error, never quietly the app's own Space), none gives the app's own. `SpaceId::owner()` names the app of an app-scoped id.
- **Stored forms and VocabVersion.** No stored form changes: `GrantKey.space` (`SpaceScope` JSON `{"kind":"only","v":"work"}`), prov's `Label` (`Confidentiality::Private(BTreeSet<SpaceId>)`) and `Address.space` keep their text; old ids parse as before. No porter code writes an app-scoped id into the registry (every grant porter makes is `SpaceScope::Any`), so no file this build writes holds a form an older daemon cannot read. VocabVersion stays 11 (kind.rs: a bump is for a new kind or field).
- **Ownership (`SpaceScope::open_to`, `GrantKey::space_is_open`, `consent::decide_key`).** An app's own Space belongs to that app: `open_to(&AppName)` is true for `Any`, `desktop` and a desktop-wide Space, and for `app:<a>:<n>` only when the name is `<a>`. The name is the key's `app`, which each transport derives from the connection (never the request, never the id's text alone). Where a scope reaches consent: no caller sends a scope today (the sheet, `Choose`, `Peer.Verdicts`, `RequestAgentGrant` and the sync switches all make `Any` keys), so the rule is enforced where a key is decided or a grant used: `decide_key` (porter-service `Registry::verdicts`/`candidates`, accountd `Peer.Verdicts`) answers `Denied` for a key over another app's own Space whatever the store holds, and never `Ask`; `choose::allow` records no such grant; `IssueToken` and both relays (`grant_account`) refuse such a stored grant. The refusal is the existing `Refusal::Denied` (the smallest that fits: "refused"; it is what an app already handles). When a later lane lets an app ask for a Space-scoped grant, the key it builds goes through the same check.
- **The registry (`org.quire.Spaces1` at `/org/quire/Spaces1`, on accountd's connection and bus name `org.quire.Accounts1`; `dbus/org.quire.Spaces1.xml`, `porter_dbus::{SpacesProxy, SpacesSkeleton, SPACES_PATH, SPACE_KEY_NAME, SPACE_KEY_LOOK, SPACE_KEY_CREATED}`).**
  - `List() -> a(sa{sv})`: id, then `name` (`s`), `look` (`s`) and `created` (`x`, Unix seconds), in the order made.
  - `Create(name s, look s) -> s`: the new id, `space-<n>` from a counter kept in the file (never from the name; an id already taken, such as an adopted one, is skipped), so a rename keeps it.
  - `Rename(id s, name s)`, `SetLook(id s, look s)`, `Remove(id s)`.
  - Signal `Changed(id s, what s)`, `what` = `created`, `renamed`, `look`, `removed`, sent one by one to each connection accountd knows (has called it; a client calls `List` once). 
  - Who: any identified caller may `List`; `Create` only `CallerRole::App`, `Settings` and `SheetHost` (never `Agent`, `Cua`, `AgentLauncher` or `PorterDaemon`: a Space is a consent scope the person makes; an assistant that should make one asks through docket and the confirming app calls `Create`; Whopper 2026-10-09); `Rename`, `SetLook`, `Remove` only `CallerRole::Settings` and `SheetHost` (as `Accounts1` checks roles: the caller table, then the role); anyone else and unknown senders get `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.AccessDenied`.
  - Bounds: a name is 1 to 64 characters, not blank, no control characters (`SpaceName`); a look is at most 1024 bytes of UTF-8, opaque (quire sends its `SpaceLook` as compact JSON; porter never parses it; empty is the default look) (`SpaceLook`). Out of bounds, or a Space that is not there: `InvalidArgs` with a plain sentence.
  - Limit: `Create` at most `accountd::CREATES_PER_WINDOW` (10) per app (`AppName`) within `accountd::CREATE_WINDOW` (60 s); more answers `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.LimitsExceeded` "Too many new Spaces at once: at most 10 a minute. Try again in a minute." Counted in memory (a restart forgets it).
  - Storage: `spaces.json` beside `registry.json` (`SpacesStore::File(<registry dir>)`; tests use `SpacesStore::Memory`), `{"version":1,"next":n,"spaces":[DesktopSpaceRecord...]}`, written through `store::file::AtomicFile`, the code fix-store gave the registry (moved out of `store.rs` and shared, not copied): a staging name of its own, synced, renamed, `spaces.json.bak` kept. A `spaces.json` that cannot be read is never written over: the list is served empty, every change answers `Failed` "The Spaces could not be saved.", and accountd logs the file's path.
  - First start (no `spaces.json`; Whopper's adjustment 1): every desktop-wide id the registry's grants are scoped to (`Only(<slug>)`, not `desktop`, not app-scoped) is adopted with that slug as its id and its name and the default look, and the file is written at once, so a later start adopts nothing again.
  - Audit (additive `AuditEvent`s, the audit file is unversioned): `space_created`, `space_renamed`, `space_removed`, each `{"space": <id>}` and the caller's app, never the name. `SetLook` is not audited.
  - `Remove` ends every grant whose scope is `Only(<that Space>)`, each through `revoke_grant` (audited `revoked`, persisted, `GrantChanged` to its holder). Apps whose Space linked to it learn by `Changed(id, "removed")` and fall back to their own Space: that is the app's choice, not porter's, and porter moves or deletes no memory (almanac's and docket's).
- **porter-client.** `DbusTransport::spaces() -> Spaces` (feature `dbus`): `list() -> Vec<DesktopSpaceRecord>`, `create(&SpaceName, &SpaceLook) -> DesktopSpace`, `watch() -> SpaceChanges` (a stream of `(DesktopSpace, SpaceChange)`; it subscribes, then calls `List` so accountd tells it). Errors are `SpacesError { TooMany, Invalid, Denied, Unreachable, Malformed }`; porter-dbus gains `is_limits_exceeded`.
- **Tests.** porter-core `space::tests` (every kind round-trips, old ids read as linked, texts of neither form refused, the app form never collides with a slug, `desktop` is not desktop-wide, `for_app_links_or_keeps_the_apps_own_space`, scopes pin their JSON), `space_record::tests` (names, looks by bytes, change words, the record's JSON), `audit::tests::the_new_events_pin_their_form`, `consent::decide::tests::another_apps_own_space_is_refused_and_shared_spaces_pass`; porter-service `tests/it` `space_owner::a_grant_over_another_apps_own_space_is_refused` (token and relay `Denied`; before the check both were served) and `space_owner::own_linked_outside_and_any_spaces_still_pass`; accountd `spaces::tests` (adoption once, minted ids across renames and restarts, not there, the per-app limit and its window, an unreadable file left alone), the `store::tests` unchanged and passing over `AtomicFile`, and bus `spaces::{any_identified_app_lists_and_creates_and_a_stranger_is_denied, only_settings_and_the_shell_rename_restyle_and_remove, bad_names_and_looks_are_invalid_args, known_connections_are_told_each_change, removing_a_space_ends_the_grants_scoped_to_it_alone, legacy_slugs_are_adopted_on_the_first_start_only, a_restart_keeps_the_spaces, an_app_making_too_many_spaces_at_once_is_held_back}`; porter-dbus `introspection::the_spaces_registry_declares_its_five_methods_and_one_signal` and the frozen-file test over `org.quire.Spaces1.xml`; porter-client `dbus_spaces::{an_app_lists_creates_and_hears_of_new_spaces, too_many_new_spaces_and_a_stranger_are_told_apart}`.
- **Open.** A Space-scoped consent ask (an app asking for a grant in one Space) does not exist yet; when it does, its key goes through `decide_key`. `Changed` reaches only connections that have called accountd. quire's kit decides what the default look is (porter stores empty).

## Lane fix-start (services answer from their first call; the last flaky tests; rel-13 follow-up, base `b903574`, 2026-10-09)

- **One start for every daemon: `porter_dbus::serve_ready(&Connection)` (verified: inferd and accountd each had a private copy of the wait, and syncd's `service::serve` claimed `org.quire.Sync1` with none).** The wait moved into porter-dbus (`src/ready.rs`, the same look: `org.freedesktop.DBus.Peer.Ping` to the connection itself through the bus, 50 ms per look, an error after 30 s) and is the one function inferd (`service::serve_on`), accountd (`core::serve_with`) and syncd (`service::object::serve`) call after their last object is registered and before `request_name`. `inferd::service::dispatching` (pub, no caller outside inferd; none in sill, detent, mailo, docket, almanac or cua by grep of origin/master) and accountd's copy are gone. porter-dbus's `tokio` moved from dev-dependencies to dependencies (for the deadline). porter's own lock is unchanged (its entry listed dev-dependencies already), but a consumer that takes porter-dbus by path lists only its normal dependencies: sill's (and detent's) `Cargo.lock` gains `"tokio"` under `porter-dbus`, so their `--locked` check needs that one lock line (for their owners). Consumer checks on `f5c769c`: almanac, docket and cua (`[patch]` with every porter crate their locks name, `--offline`, each crate seen built from the lane's worktree) pass; sill `--locked` is refused for that lock line, and with `--offline` stops in `sill-settings` on quire's `Schema` (no field `foreign`), the check's quire sibling (`quire-0.2.13`, as the brief sets it) being older than sill's own pin, not porter. A consumer that takes porter by git gets the line when it moves its rev. The test-side fakes that serve an object and then claim a name do the same: accountd's sheet host in `tests/it/common` and `tests/it/google.rs`, syncd's fake accountd in `tests/it/wipe_bus.rs` (a daemon's call to a fake still starting was dropped the same way). Test: `syncd::it start::a_call_made_as_soon_as_the_name_is_owned_is_answered`, built like inferd's and accountd's (the object server's runtime held back 2 s; a syncd that claims first drops the call and the test's 10 s wait fails; with the wait it passes in about 2.4 s). Left (other lanes' files): porter-rig's fake Secret Service (`secrets_service.rs`), inferd's fake accountd (`tests/it/hosting/accountd.rs`) and porter-client's fake daemons (`tests/it/common/inferd.rs`, `dbus_open.rs`, `dbus_sheets.rs`) claim their names without the wait; each can call `porter_dbus::serve_ready` first.
- **Every object before the name (verified for inferd only: its settings module was served by `main` after `serve_on` had claimed the name, so a Settings call in that window was `UnknownObject`).** New `inferd::service::serve_with_settings(connection, daemon, settings)` registers `Inference1` (and the agent endpoints), then the settings module, then waits and claims; `main` calls it (`serve_on` stays for the tests that serve no module). Order checked in the other two: accountd registers Manager, Grants, Tokens, Peer, `Spaces1` (lane spaces, at `/org/quire/Spaces1`), the account objects and its settings module, and starts its roster watch, all before the claim (fix-hang's note that accountd's module came after the name is not so); syncd registers the Photos picker (when on) in `main` before `service::serve`, which registers `Sync1` and then claims. Test: `inferd::it settings_module::the_settings_module_answers_a_call_made_as_soon_as_the_name_is_owned` (a Settings caller calls `Describe` the moment the bus says the name is owned; under the old order that call raced the module's registration and was answered `UnknownObject` whenever it won; the test cannot force the old order's loss, so it fails only when the call wins).
- **`process_credential::a_launcher_that_leaves_ends_its_credentials_and_only_its_own` (verified, a daemon race, not the test's).** It failed once in fix-hang's runs with `left: [] right: [LauncherGone]`: the file had gone but the audit had no `ProcessCredentialRevoked`. `Credentials::end_where` (and `take`) unlinked a credential's file inside the call, and the callers in `handoff.rs` wrote the audit line afterwards, so a reader who saw the file gone (the test, or a launcher watching its file) could find no line saying why. Now both take a `note` that is called, with the lock released, before the file goes, and every end path (launcher gone, grant revoked or account removed, session closed, `RevokeProcessCredential`) audits through it; a credential taken back unissued (its grant went while the key was read) is still not audited. The signal to the launcher still follows the line. Test: `accountd credentials::tests::the_end_is_noted_while_the_file_is_still_there` (the note sees the file still there, for `end_where` and for `take`; under the old order there was no note before the unlink, and the audit came after it).
- **`bus_sheets::a_caller_that_closes_the_request_takes_the_host_sheet_down` (verified, a daemon race, not the test's: 5 in fix-hang's 500 stress runs, "timed out waiting for the host to be told to close").** The test waits until the host has logged `Open`, then closes the Request. The host logs the call as it receives it, before its reply reaches accountd; `Request.Close` aborts the sheet's task, and if that happened while `BusSheets::open_link` was still awaiting `Open`'s reply, the flow was dropped before the `BusLink` existed, and only `BusLink`'s `Drop` sent `Close`. So the host kept showing a sheet nobody would answer: a real bug for a person whose app gives up on a sheet just as it appears. Now the handle is held in a `Shown` guard from the moment `Open` is sent; its `Drop` sends `Close` (the link owns it once `Open` returns). A host that refused `Open` is also sent `Close` for that handle; its answer is not read. Test: `accountd::it bus_sheets::a_caller_that_closes_the_request_while_open_is_on_its_way_back_takes_the_sheet_down` (the test host's `Open` returns 2 s after it logs the sheet, `SheetHost::slow_to_return`; the Request is closed in that window; before the fix nothing ever told the host, so the 5 s wait fails every time; with it the host is told at once).
- **`syncd::it confirm_bus::the_shell_hears_a_hold_and_its_end_but_no_data_of_a_dataset_it_does_not_own` hung to the 120 s limit (found in this lane's runs, 1 in 10; a test race).** The test emptied the replica by removing its three files one by one and then dropping its history, expecting the engine's next look to be a full listing that lacks all three (a hold). An engine poll that landed between two removals took them as plain deletions: no hold came (the 120 s wait), or a smaller one. The test's replica now makes the engine's listing wait while the test changes it (a lock around `changes`, held by `emptied` across the removals and the compaction), so the engine sees the replica before or after, as a server would show it. All three hold tests in the file share `emptied`. `next_hold` waits 60 s (it waited 120 s, as long as the harness, so a miss showed as a timeout with no name).
- **`syncd::it photos_picker_bus::only_the_photos_app_may_call_and_every_bad_word_or_missing_picker_is_a_typed_error` (found in this lane's runs, 5 in 300; a test race).** It dropped the fake Google and expected the very next `Poll` to fail `Unavailable`, but it answered `"waiting"`. Dropping `Running` aborts the fake's task, and that abort (and the abort of each connection task it held) takes effect when the runtime next reaches it, not at the drop; a request on the connection syncd keeps open can be served in between. The test now polls until the answer is an error (60 s bound, which names itself) and checks it is `Unavailable`; syncd's own behaviour is unchanged. (A `Running::stop().await` in porter-fake-servers that waits for the tasks would make every such test exact; that crate is not this lane's.)
- **`syncd::it pim_bus::account_removed_removes_the_mirror_and_it_does_not_come_back` (found in this lane's runs, 3 in 300, "never happened within 60s: the account's mirror is gone"; a daemon race).** The PIM supervisor's look writes each running collection's `displayname` and `color` (`vdir::write_meta`) outside the engine's cycle lock, and `write_meta` made the directory when it was missing. An `AccountRemoved` wipe (it stops the account's engines and deletes their mirrors) landing between the supervisor's listing of the collections and those writes brought the account's directory back, with the name files in it, and nothing removed it again: a removed account left a stub mirror. `write_meta` no longer makes the directory (`PimMirror::open` makes it when a collection starts); a gone directory is `NotFound`, logged as a failed look, and the next look retires the collection. Test: `syncd datasets::pim::vdir::tests::metadata_never_makes_a_collection_whose_directory_is_gone` (before the fix the write made `account/personal` and succeeded).
- **`syncd::it gdrive_bus::the_changes_cursor_survives_a_restart_and_an_expired_one_reconciles_without_re_uploads` (found in this lane's runs, 3 in 600, "nothing was uploaded again: left 2, right 1"; a test race).** With the requests printed (the assertion now names the drive's files and every request) the second upload was an update of `mine.txt` with the same bytes, right after the restart. The test wrote `mine.txt` with `std::fs::write` (truncate, then write) while the engine was running: a scan between the two saw an empty file and recorded that hash, the upload read the file a moment later and sent `mine`, so the drive looked right and the test went on; the next scan (after the restart) found `mine` against the recorded empty hash and sent it again. The test now puts its files in place in one step (`write_whole`: staged in the scratch directory, then renamed), as an editor saving a file does; the other upload-counting test (`big.bin`, `small.txt`) too. Noted, not changed (the engine and the `Dataset` seam, not this lane's): the journal keeps the scan's hash for an upload, not the hash of the bytes sent, so a file caught mid-write by a scan is sent once more on the next cycle; the end state is right, at the cost of one upload.
- **Runs** (`~/rs-wt/fix-start/stress.sh`, fix-hang's script on this lane's archive, the gate's jail, `--stress-count 300`, filter `binary_id(accountd::it) | binary_id(syncd::it)`). Before (fix-hang's runs, accountd with inferd): `bus_sheets` 4 in 200 and 1 in 300, `process_credential` 1 in 200. Here, with the accountd fixes and the shared start but before the syncd test fixes: 10 iterations, one `confirm_bus` hang; then 300 iterations, 290 passed (accountd none; syncd `photos_picker_bus` 5, `pim_bus` 3, `gdrive_bus` 2); with those fixed, 300 iterations, 299 passed (`gdrive_bus` once, which its new message diagnosed). On the final tip (rebased on master `993c56b`, 239 tests an iteration): **300 iterations, 300 passed, no failure and no timeout.**

## Lane fix-skeleton (no reachable `todo!()`, audit2 idiom-1, batch 8; base `e3d8f2c`, 2026-10-09)

- **idiom-1 (verified).** A person's provider file in their own folder with a new id and `[auth] kind = "oauth_mints_key", issuer = "openrouter"` (the file's spelling of `AuthKind::OAuthMintsKey`; the audit's `o_auth_mints_key` is not a kind and fails to parse, so the file is skipped) mapped to `FamilyProvider::OpenRouter` in `accountd::providers::family_of`, whose every method was `todo!()`. Now `family_of` does not map `OAuthMintsKey`, so the spec lands in `unserved` with its logged reason and is neither served nor listed. Adding it by id is the existing unknown-provider refusal: nothing is asked and nothing is stored.
- **The skeleton (`porter-families` `family_skeleton!`).** Every method answers a plain refusal instead of panicking: `discover`, `open`, `sign_in` and the session's `access_token` give `ProviderError::Forbidden`; `revoke` gives `RevokeOutcome::Unsupported`; `renewed` gives `None`; the sign-in's `next` gives `SignInStep::Failed(SignInFault::Forbidden)`. The provider's `readiness` is `NeedsClient`, so it is never `Ready` and the add list never shows it.
- **Other `todo!()`s (none reachable today).** A grep of `todo!`/`unimplemented!` over `crates/` finds no production code left with one, apart from the two refused in the second commit: `porter-infer`'s `Broker::infer` and `inferd`'s `SpeechRunner::speak`. No bus call, file or network path reaches either. `Broker` is re-exported by `porter-infer`, but nothing outside tests calls `infer`; nothing calls `speak`. Speech answers `ModelError::NotReady`; the broker answers `InferReply::Refused(InferRefusal::Unavailable)` and sends no event. Not changed: a stale comment in `crates/porter-client/tests/it/socket.rs` (about line 120) says `add_account` is `todo!()` in porter-service; it is not (`porter-service/src/add.rs` has it). The lane that owns that test should drop the comment.
- **Tests.** `accountd` unit `providers::tests::a_persons_file_asking_for_an_unbuilt_family_is_not_served_and_names_why`; `accountd` `tests/it` `add::adding_a_persons_provider_of_an_unbuilt_family_is_refused` (no panic, nothing asked, nothing stored); `porter-families` `tests/it/skeleton.rs` `every_method_of_an_unbuilt_family_refuses_and_none_panics` and `an_unbuilt_family_is_never_ready_so_the_add_list_never_shows_it` (feature `openrouter`); `porter-families` unit `openrouter::tests::the_unbuilt_session_and_sign_in_refuse_instead_of_panicking`; `porter-fake` `tests/it/fakes.rs` `the_unbuilt_broker_refuses_instead_of_panicking`. Checked by reasoning about each input, not by reverting: the accountd tests drive `family_of`'s `OpenRouter` arm, which panicked on the first `todo!()` the add flow reached; the skeleton tests call the methods that were `todo!()`.
- **Vocabulary and wire.** None. No enum, reply, bus method or stored form changed; VocabVersion is untouched.
- **Consumers.** No public item was removed. `family_of` sends that spec to `unserved`; no consumer outside porter matches on `FamilyProvider::OpenRouter` (checked by grep of the porter crates; the other consumers were not grepped in this lane).

## Lane fix-io (one way to write a file, fewer copies; audit2 dead-6, dead-10, idiom-3, idiom-8, idiom-11; base `e3d8f2c`, 2026-10-09)

- **dead-6 (verified).** One atomic writer, `porter_core::atomic` (porter-core is the lowest crate every user reaches; check-boundary only forbids dependencies, and the module is the crate's one use of `std::fs`, said in its lib doc). `AtomicWrite::{PRIVATE, SHARED}` (owner-only or default mode; `.in_existing_dir()` never makes a directory) with `write`, `copy`, `stage`, `commit`: a staging file `.tmp-<pid>-<n>.tmp` beside the target (a dot file, so no scan lists it; `create_new`), written, `sync_all`, renamed, directory synced; a failure removes the staging file. `AtomicFile` (the registry's file with its `.bak`) moved there from accountd `store/file.rs`. Converted: accountd `store.rs`, `spaces.rs`, `settings.rs write_client` (now owner-only: it holds client secrets); inferd `cloud/spend.rs` (owner-only), `settings/file.rs`; porter-secrets `file.rs`; syncd `photos/cas.rs` (`write_atomic`, `copy_atomic` stay as one-line calls) and `pim/vdir.rs`. porter-rig's test writer is untouched. vdir's write now syncs the file and the directory (it did neither): a sync cannot be seen from outside the process, so `porter-core atomic::tests::a_write_reaches_the_disk_before_it_is_published_and_the_directory_after` counts the syncs of the writer vdir calls (a thread-local counter, test builds only); vdir's own test checks it still never brings back a removed collection's directory (`NotFound`). Cost: every mirror file now pays two fsyncs. Changed on purpose: a stale `.tmp` of porter-secrets' key file from a crash is no longer overwritten by the next save (unique names, as the registry's always were); its test says so.
- **dead-10 (two of three).** `HttpResponse::retry_after_seconds` (porter-http) replaces five copies (storage-webdav, -graph, -gdrive, syncd's Graph source, and syncd's Photos `api.rs`); test `porter-http message::tests::retry_after_is_whole_seconds_or_nothing`. `porter_bridge::ENGINE_RETRY` is the one policy of inferd's `runner.rs` and porter-client's `engines/wire.rs`. **DISPATCH_BOUND: already done** by fix-start: `porter_dbus::serve_ready`'s single `BOUND` is the only copy (grep of accountd, inferd and syncd finds no other); nothing changed.
- **idiom-3.** `removal::wipe` runs its `remove_dir_all` loop in `spawn_blocking`; `pim/mirrors.rs retire` goes through pim's `blocking` (now `pub(super)`). No new test: the behaviour is the same and the bus tests that wipe and retire cover it; a test of "does not block the runtime" would be timing-based.
- **idiom-8.** `porter_dbus::{load_callers, table_from_file, table_from_toml, CallerFileError}` (module `callers_file`, cargo feature `callers-file`, off by default so a consumer of porter-dbus gains no dependency; accountd and syncd turn it on) replace both `callers_file.rs`; the tests of accountd's copy (the superset) moved with it. `accountd` still re-exports the four names; `syncd::callers_file` is gone (`porter_dbus::load_callers`). syncd's `toml` is now a dev-dependency. `porter_core::xdg` (`Xdg`, `PathError` with thiserror, `absolute`) is the one XDG resolver for accountd `paths.rs`, syncd `paths.rs` and inferd `config.rs`; both daemons' `PathError` is now that type (same variant `NoHome`, same text). A behaviour change in inferd: it now ignores a relative or empty XDG variable as the other two did (test `a_relative_or_empty_xdg_variable_is_ignored`; before, they placed files under the working directory).
- **idiom-11.** `accountd spaces.rs create`: the id search is `next..=u64::MAX` and the counter advances with `checked_add`; a file whose counter is at the top is refused (`Unsaved`), never wrapped or panicking (test `a_counter_at_the_top_of_its_range_is_refused_and_not_wrapped`, which fails without the fix: `n + 1` overflows). The `ok_or(Unsaved)` there was dead only because the range was unbounded; with the bound it is the full-counter answer, so it stays. `porter-service choose.rs next_grant_id` searches `held + 1` numbers (one is free by counting), gdrive `boundary_for` and the Photos picker's `unique` search `..=u32::MAX`. No test for these three: reaching the end needs 2^32 names.

## Lane cleanup-dead (mechanical removals, second audit; base `e3d8f2c`, 2026-10-09)

The old slugs `llm`, `speech_in`, `speech_out` still read (serde and `Slot::from_slug` aliases). Consumers: none in code of sill, detent, mailo, docket, almanac, cua. **For Whopper, quire design docs that name `AiKind`** (not edited here): `design/31-ACCOUNTS.md` lines 146 and 801; `design/agent/SPEC.md` lines 204 and 323; `design/agent/models.md` lines 584, 585, 587, 591 and 594 (in those three files `AiKind` is the design's own enum, not the porter alias; Whopper decides whether to rename them to `Slot`).
- **dead-3 (done): `org.quire.Photos1.Picker` is no longer served or declared by default.** It sits behind a cargo feature `photos-picker`, off by default, in porter-dbus (the introspection and the module) and syncd (which also needs `SYNCD_PHOTOS=on`). The code and its tests stay; the Sync1 introspection test checks the default surface, and the picker's surface when the feature is on. `dbus/org.quire.Sync1.xml` no longer lists the Picker interface. **Desktop:** nothing in v1 calls the Picker (no Photos app, D6/D7). The desktop must not call `org.quire.Photos1.Picker` in the default build; a build with `--features photos-picker` and `SYNCD_PHOTOS=on` serves it again.
- **dead-4 (done): `Dialect::Vllm` is removed** (porter-client `engines/engine.rs`); the `engines` feature stays (off by default, no consumer yet).

- **dead-8 (done): google `reauth::testing_expires` is renamed `testing_expiry_seam`** (`pub`, re-exported from porter-families; its callers are its test and the re-export). Consumers of `testing_expires`: none on origin/master.

- **Build**: `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo check --workspace --all-features --tests` clean; `cargo check -p syncd --tests` (default features) passes.
- **Follow-up, not fixed here (pre-existing, found by the default-features check)**: porter-client `transport/framed.rs` lines 60 and 94 (`write_body`, `read_body_with_fds`) are dead code in the default features (a warning, not an error). The file is not this lane's.

## Lane fix-client (porter-client and sign-in; idiom-2, idiom-6, idiom-7, idiom-12, dead-5; base `e3d8f2c`, rebased on fix-io's `7f96357`, 2026-10-09)

- **idiom-2 (verified): a cancelled turn's events could land in the next turn.** `EngineSession` shared one channel between turns; cancel aborted the task without waiting, so on a multi-thread runtime the old task could still send after the next turn began. Each turn now owns its channel (`Turn { task, inbox }`); cancel drops the turn, receiver included, so anything the old task still sends goes nowhere. The session's own events (`Routed`, a refusal, `Cancelled`) stay on the session's channel, read first; only a turn's own `Finished` ends the turn. Test: `engines::a_cancelled_turn_never_reaches_the_next_one` (4 worker threads, 60 rounds of cancel-then-new-turn; with the old shared channel an "old" delta or the old `Finished` would show among the new turn's events). 30 repeated runs and one full porter-client run passed.
- **idiom-6 (verified): `#[non_exhaustive]` on `ClientError`, `TransportError`, `SpacesError`, `LauncherError`, `ChildKeyError` only.** `ClientError::Refused` no longer prints the refusal with Debug: a plain sentence per refusal (the match names every `Refusal`, so a new one cannot go unsaid). Consumer impact (origin/master of sill, detent, mailo, docket, almanac, cua, quire; anyview and temor have none): detent `add_io.rs`, mailo `link/client.rs`, docket `docket-launch/src/dbus.rs` (LauncherError) and `voiced/src/link.rs` have a wildcard arm; docket's `SpacesError` and other `TransportError` uses only name the type. **almanac `crates/memoryd/src/infer/embed.rs:191` (`transport_failure`) matches `TransportError` with no wildcard and will stop compiling: it needs a `_` arm.** sill, cua and quire have no match on these porter-client enums (their hits are docket-client, almanac-client or sill's own types, or design docs).
- **idiom-7 (verified): errors that kept only text now keep their source.** accountd `providers::SkipReason` (unreadable / malformed / name is not id / would widen) in place of `String`; inferd `peers::TableError`, `ConfigError::Toml(toml::de::Error)` and a `RunError` for `main`; syncd `pim::mirrors::OpenError` (journal / mirror / rewrite / name taken); `ChildKeyError::Unreadable(io::ErrorKind)`. Skipped: accountd's and syncd's `callers_file` (fix-io moved it into porter-dbus). porter-secrets' `map_err(|_| ..)` is left as it is. The log text of each is the same sentence as before.
- **idiom-12 (verified): the key file is read into one wiped buffer.** `credential.rs` `read_all_at` preads into a `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` of at most 16 KiB plus one byte (it never grows, so no stale copy; a longer file is `Unreadable(InvalidData)`). The Nextcloud Login Flow v2 poll token is a `SecretText` (Debug prints no value). Tests: `credential::tests::a_key_file_over_the_limit_is_unreadable_and_one_at_the_limit_is_read`, and the flow test checks Debug does not show the token. porter-client gains `zeroize` (one lock line).
- **dead-5 (decided: deleted).** accountd cannot tell that no browser is available: the terminal host's `open_browser` spawns `xdg-open` and drops the result, and the sheet's `OpenBrowser` step has no way to report a failure back. So there is nothing to choose a device code on. Removed `SignInFlow`, `MicrosoftEnv::with_flow` and the `flow` field, `begin_device`, `await_device`, the device state and the three device-code sign-in tests (the reauth-grant note above names the one that went). `porter-oauth`'s device-code functions and the fake issuer's device grant stay (porter-oauth's own tests use them). `SignInStep::ShowCode` stays in porter-core (agent login uses it).

## Lane places (where the assistant may run; docket's ask and detent's, base `abcf48c`, 2026-10-09)

docket lets the person choose where the assistant runs (this computer: always; their own computers: once added; signed-in cloud accounts: off until turned on, or ask each time) and sends inferd only the allowed set. This lane gives docket the ids, the list, the routing inside a set and a typed refusal, and gives Settings (detent) the means to add and remove a computer.

**API for docket (porter-infer, `porter_infer::*`).**
- `PlaceId` (checked string newtype): `this-computer`, `computer:<name>`, `account:<account id>`. `PlaceId::{this_computer(), computer(&ComputerName), account(&AccountId), parse(&str)}`, `as_str()`, `kind() -> PlaceKind`, `target() -> PlaceTarget`. A bad text is `CoreError::MalformedId { what: "place id", .. }` (also from serde).
- `PlaceKind { ThisComputer, OwnComputer, CloudAccount }` has no payload, slugs `this_computer`, `own_computer`, `cloud_account` (`slug()`, `from_slug()`). **Deviation from the brief:** the brief gave `kind()` payloads (`OwnComputer(ComputerName)`) and also `would_need: Option<PlaceKind>` carried as a slug. One type cannot be both, so the payload view is `PlaceTarget { ThisComputer, OwnComputer(ComputerName), CloudAccount(AccountId) }` (`PlaceId::target()`), and `PlaceKind` is the slug-only kind. `ComputerName` is the id grammar (lowercase `[a-z0-9]` then `[a-z0-9._-]`, at most 64).
- `PlaceRow { id, kind, name, provider: Option<String>, models: Vec<PlaceModel { id: ModelId, name }>, state: PlaceState { Ready, NotReady } }` (the bus `ready` boolean, data only).
- `OpenOptions` gains `places: Option<Vec<PlaceId>>` (absent is not empty) and `place_models: BTreeMap<PlaceId, ModelId>`; builders `with_places`, `with_place_model`. New serde keys skip when absent, so the old JSON is unchanged. `OpenOptions` has pub fields: a struct literal without `..Default::default()` breaks (none in docket or almanac; porter-rig's one was fixed).
- `NoPlaceReason { NotReady, FloorRefused, ModelNotOffered, NoneCapable }` (`slug()`, `name()` Pascal, `from_name()`, `ALL`; the order is the order they win), `PlaceRefusal { reason, would_need: Option<PlaceKind> }`.

**Bus API (`org.quire.Inference1`, all additive: no existing member, argument or error changed, nothing in porter-core's wire types, so no `VocabVersion` bump; the skeleton, proxy, `dbus/org.quire.Inference1.xml` and `porter-dbus` `inference1_xml_unchanged_members` changed in the same commits, as for `Sync1.ConfirmDiscard`).**
- `Places() -> a(sa{sv})`: one row per place known now, the place id first, in this order: this computer, the person's own computers by name, cloud accounts in accountd's order. Keys (`porter_dbus::PLACE_KEY_*`): `kind` s, `name` s (the computer's name as the person gave it, or the account's label; this computer is `This computer`), `provider` s (cloud accounts only: the provider's display name, else its id), `models` a(ss) (model id, display name: the **language** models the place can serve now; for an account only while it works), `ready` b. A cloud account is an account whose provider reaches a hosted model of the catalogue; a local runtime (Ollama...) is `this-computer`. A computer is `ready` when one of its models answered the look made when `Places` is called (attached engines are looked at then); an account when its state is not `needs_reauth`, `needs_login` or `offline`.
- Who may call `Places`: Settings (its unit), the shell (`sill-shell.scope`, role `Shell`) and docket's `companiond.service`, by unit with the MainPID rule (`ProcCallers`, as accountd's rows). Everyone else, apps included: `AccessDenied`. The shell has no other inferd right (Open, Prepare, Availability, Usage, Rescan: `AccessDenied`).
- `EnginesChanged` also fires when a cloud AI account appears, goes or changes state. accountd tells a joined porter daemon `AccountAdded`, `AccountRemoved`, `CapabilityChanged` and the `State` property change (`hub::daemon_hears`; not grants, which are per app); inferd joins the roster by one `Peer.Verdicts` for its own app (`account_news.rs`) and again whenever accountd comes back on the bus. `Peer.Verdicts` rows gain the details `label`, `provider_label` and `state` (additive keys; an older accountd leaves them out and inferd then lists the account with its id as name and as ready).
- `Open`, `Prepare`, `Availability` take `options["places"]` (`as`, `OPTION_PLACES`) and `options["place_models"]` (`a{ss}`, `OPTION_PLACE_MODELS`; only with `places`, and only for places in it). Only the units `companiond.service`, `readerd.service`, `intentd.service` may send either (role `Placer`); from any other caller the call is `AccessDenied`, not ignored. A text that is not a place id, a pin outside the set, or a pin without `places`: `InvalidArgs`. Without them routing is exactly as it was (pinned by `without_places_routing_is_as_it_always_was_and_with_them_it_is_the_callers`).
- Routing in a set (`router::placed`): the floor, local-only, consent, spend and the person's picks apply at each place; the places are tried in the caller's order and the first that can serve wins (within a place the usual order: the person's pick, then price). A pin is the caller's word for that place and overrides the person's pick there. Only a model that can serve now counts (`Ready`, `Loading`, `Loadable`; a cloud model while its account works). Never outside the set. A voice chat's stages stay in the place the session was routed to.
- The refusal: when nothing in the set can serve, `NoAllowedPlace` with one reason. If several hold the first of `NotReady`, `FloorRefused`, `ModelNotOffered`, `NoneCapable` is named; a consent or spend refusal (`NeedsGrant`, `Denied`, `OverBudget`) of a place in the set is told as it always was, not as a place reason. `ModelNotOffered` is also the answer when the person's own pick for this kind of work names a model the place lacks. `would_need` is the kind of place **outside** the set that the usual rules would have picked, if any could serve now (so it respects the floor, local-only and consent).
- **How the refusal is carried.** zbus can return an error with a structured body, so it is: the error name is `org.quire.Inference1.Error.NoAllowedPlace.{NotReady,FloorRefused,ModelNotOffered,NoneCapable}` (`porter_dbus::PLACE_ERROR_PREFIX`), and the body is two strings: the plain sentence (where every client already reads an error's text) and the `would_need` slug (`this_computer`, `own_computer`, `cloud_account`, or empty). `porter_dbus::place_refusal_of(&zbus::Error) -> Option<(reason name, Option<slug>)>` reads it. No prose is parsed and no first-line convention is needed. `Open` answers it before any session exists; a refusal that arises later in the session is still the session's `Finished(Refused(Unavailable))`. `Prepare` and `Availability` answer it as the error (they answered a refusal slug before, which still holds for the other refusals).
- porter-client (`infer`): `ClientError::NoAllowedPlace { reason, would_need }` (not `InferRefused::NoAllowedPlace`: adding a variant to `InferRefusal` would break every exhaustive match, and `ClientError` is `#[non_exhaustive]`; confirmed it already is, with `TransportError`, `SpacesError`, `LauncherError`, `ChildKeyError`), `TransportError::NoAllowedPlace` for transports, and `Accounts::places() -> Vec<PlaceRow>` with `Transport::places()` (default `Unreachable`, so an outside implementor compiles). `From<TransportError> for ClientError` is now written by hand (`NoAllowedPlace` becomes the client variant, the rest `Transport`); `ClientError::Transport` no longer carries `#[from]`.

**Caller rows added (`dist/inferd.toml`, `[callers]`).** `shell = ["sill-shell.scope"]` (role `Shell`); `places = ["companiond.service", "readerd.service", "intentd.service"]` (each must already be an app under `[callers.apps]`, else the table is refused; they become role `Placer`, which Opens exactly as an app does and may send `places`); of these only the companion (app `org.quire.Companion`) may also call `Places`. Settings keeps its `settings` row. `CallerTable` gains `with_shell`, `with_places`; `TableError` gains `BadShellEntry`, `PlacesNotAnApp`. accountd's `callers.toml` is unchanged (the shell row was already there).

**Adding and removing a computer (detent).** Attached engines had no machine name, only a model id per table. `[engines.attached."<model id>"]` gains an optional `computer = "<name>"`; engines that share it are one place `computer:<name>`; an engine on another machine (`where = "my-network"`) that names none belongs to `other-computer`. So the id of a place maps to the engines by that key.
- `AddComputer(name s, models a(sa{sv})) -> s`: `name` is what the person typed (1 to 64 characters; the id's name is made from it: lowercase letters and digits, runs of anything else one dash; the typed text is kept and shown as `name` in `Places`). Each model is `(catalogue model id, {socket s | port q, key s?})`, `socket` the full path of the connection on this computer that leads to the engine, `port` the port on this computer that does (exactly one), `key` the engine's key if it wants one. Answers the place id (`computer:<name>`). At most 32 models; a model can be on one computer only.
- `RemoveComputer(name s)`: `name` is the name or the place id. Both Settings only (its unit): the shell, the companion, apps and everyone else get `AccessDenied`. `EnginesChanged` follows each.
- inferd never writes the hand-written file. What Settings adds is `$XDG_STATE_HOME/quire/inferd/computers.toml` (the one place the unit may write: `StateDirectory=quire/inferd`; "beside" the config is not writable under `ProtectHome=read-only`), written with `AtomicWrite::PRIVATE`, and read with the hand-written file at start; a name or model in both is the hand-written one (the added one is left out with a log line). A file that does not read is moved aside (`computers.toml.unusable`) and the list starts empty. Keys are one file each in `computer-keys/<name>--<model>.key`, `AtomicWrite::PRIVATE` (0600), referenced from the list, read at each connect as any key file; a key never appears in a reply, a log line, `Debug` output or the list (`the_key_is_in_no_debug_output_no_error_and_no_file_but_its_own`, and a file scan in the bus test).
- Every check of the hand-written file (`AttachedEntry::check`) and of the catalogue applies. Refusals are `org.quire.Inference1.Error.Computer.<Name>` (`COMPUTER_ERROR_PREFIX`), the reply text the sentence, in these exact words (`“` and `”` are the curly quotes, `{id}` the model id as sent):
  - `BadName`: Give the computer a name that has some letters or numbers in it.
  - `NoModels`: Add at least one model for this computer.
  - `TooMany`: That is more models than one computer can have here.
  - `AlreadyThere`: A computer with that name is already there. (added, or written by hand)
  - `UnknownModel`: The assistant does not know a model called “{id}”.
  - `ModelNotUsable`: The model “{id}” cannot be used from another computer.
  - `ModelTaken`: The model “{id}” is already set up on a computer.
  - `BadAddress`: “{id}” cannot be reached that way. Give the full path of a connection on this computer, or a port on this computer. (none or both given, a relative or too long path, port 0)
  - `BadKey`: The key for “{id}” cannot be used. (empty, over 4096 bytes, or more than one line)
  - `NotSaved`: The computer could not be saved.
  - `NotThere`: There is no computer with that name.
  - `AddedByHand`: That computer was added by hand in the settings file, so it can only be removed there.
  - `Unavailable`: Adding computers is not available here.
  A value of the wrong D-Bus type is `InvalidArgs`, not one of these. Nothing refused changes the list, the keys or the engines.
- Not done: porter-client has no `add_computer`/`remove_computer` (Settings calls the proxy: `InferenceProxy::{add_computer, remove_computer}`); a computer's engine is not looked at when it is added (it shows `ready` false in `Places` until it answers); the keys arrive as ordinary strings in the message and in the daemon's memory until the call ends (the `SecretText` is wiped when dropped, the bus message is not).

**Residuals.** Same-uid trust as accountd's rows: `sill-shell.scope` can be started by another program of the person's while the shell is not running, and then reads `Places`, which holds names and model ids, no secret. `Places` asks accountd for the accounts as the calling app (`Verdicts` for that app, class `prompt`), so the list does not depend on any app's grants. Cloud accounts whose provider the catalogue does not reach are not places. The `places` option is not read from the latchkey socket's `OpenFrame` (inferd serves the bus only; the in-process and socket transports of porter-client carry the field and ignore it).

**Tests.** porter-infer: `place::tests::{every_text_form_round_trips_through_its_typed_view, a_text_that_is_no_place_is_refused, the_reasons_have_names_that_read_back_and_slugs_that_are_their_serde_form, open_options_carry_places_and_pins_and_a_default_adds_nothing_to_the_json}`. inferd units: `router::placed::tests::*` (order, hand over, outside never used, each reason with and without `would_need`, precedence, pin, other refusals, dedupe), `peers::tests::{the_places_rows_make_placers_the_shell_row_makes_the_shell_and_the_rest_stay_apps, only_settings_the_shell_and_the_companion_list_places_and_only_placers_choose_them, a_places_unit_that_is_no_app_and_a_shell_entry_that_is_no_unit_are_refused}`, `attached::computers::tests::*`. accountd `hub::tests` (`daemon_hears`). Bus (jailed gate), `inferd` `tests/it/places.rs`: `settings_lists_this_computer_a_named_computer_and_the_cloud_accounts`, `a_computer_that_does_not_answer_is_listed_but_not_ready`, `only_settings_the_shell_and_the_companion_may_list_the_places`, `the_shell_may_list_the_places_and_ask_for_no_model`, `an_account_that_appears_makes_inferd_say_engines_changed`; `routing::{without_places_routing_is_as_it_always_was_and_with_them_it_is_the_callers, a_cloud_account_outside_the_set_is_never_used_even_when_it_is_the_only_one_able, callers_that_may_not_choose_places_are_refused_not_ignored, a_places_option_that_is_not_what_the_interface_says_is_invalid_args}`; `refusals::{not_ready_names_the_cloud_account_that_could_have_served_and_it_is_not_used, not_ready_without_a_place_outside_that_could_serve_has_no_would_need, floor_refused_when_the_class_may_not_leave_and_would_need_follows_the_floor, floor_refused_has_no_would_need_when_nothing_outside_could_serve, model_not_offered_when_the_pinned_model_is_not_at_the_place, none_capable_when_no_allowed_place_does_this_kind_of_work, an_empty_set_serves_nothing, the_reply_is_an_error_named_for_the_reason_with_plain_words_and_the_kind_in_its_body, a_set_that_can_serve_is_not_refused}` (each through porter-client, `Prepare` and `Open`); `computers::{settings_adds_a_computer_that_places_lists_and_the_assistant_uses_with_its_key, removing_a_computer_takes_it_and_its_key_away_and_says_engines_changed, what_is_refused_reaches_settings_as_a_named_error_with_a_plain_sentence, a_computer_written_by_hand_is_listed_but_cannot_be_removed_or_added_again, only_settings_may_add_or_remove_a_computer}`; `dist::the_shipped_table_names_the_placers_by_unit_and_the_shell_by_its_scope`. porter-dbus `inference1_xml_unchanged_members`.

## Lane fix-loadflake (tests that failed only on a loaded machine; base `8ac5d6f`, 2026-10-09)

Evidence: the gate logs of 2026-10-09 (load 50 to 150, swap full). Tests ran 120 to 320 s that take under a second idle; the machine, not a logic error, was the common cause. Product first, then the tests.

**Product (one commit each).**
- `serve_ready` (porter-dbus): it sent a Ping, cancelled it after 50 ms, sent the next, and gave up after 30 s. On a starved machine an answer takes longer than 50 ms, so a cancelled look was never answered and the daemon exited at 30 s ("cannot serve"). Now every look stays running (gaps 50 ms doubling to 1 s), the first answer wins, and the bound is five minutes. Tests: `ready::tests::{an_answer_slower_than_the_gap_between_looks_is_still_taken, a_start_that_takes_a_minute_is_waited_for_and_one_that_never_comes_ends}` (paused clock).
- syncd supervisors (pim, storage): a look that could not ask accountd, or could not start a mirror, waited the whole ten-minute rescan before trying again. `tick` now says whether it was whole; `spawn` waits `scheduler::next_look` (5 s, doubling, never over the rescan) after a failed look, and logs "trying again soon". Tests: `backoff::tests::a_failed_look_is_repeated_in_seconds_and_backs_off_to_the_rescan`, `retry_bus::a_look_that_could_not_ask_accountd_says_it_was_not_whole_and_the_next_one_is`.
- syncd `Hub::stop`/`stop_account` waited 30 s for a cycle in flight and then went on, so the caller deleted the mirror under the writer. Now 300 s. Test: `hub_tests::a_stop_waits_for_a_cycle_that_runs_for_minutes_on_a_starved_machine`.

**Causes, by failure.**
- accountd `binary::*` and `file_keys::test_build::*`: slowness. accountd does not touch the Secret Service at start (`Oo7Secrets` is a unit struct that connects per call); "accountd: secret store: the Secret Service" is the only line a successful start prints, so every failure's stderr ends there. Nothing before `request_name` blocks on it. The test waited 250 x 20 ms (about 5 s) for the process to claim its name; fixed in the tests.
- syncd `*_bus` ("never happened"): slowness. `eventually` counted 3000 sleeps; a starved timer made that 190 to 320 s, and the daemon's own poll cycle (one second here) had not finished.
