The "cannot write the metadata: No such file or directory" line comes AFTER the test's panic in the log (choice-scores log, lines 2544 and 2546): the failing test dropped its scratch directory while syncd's tasks were still running, so it is teardown, not a race in a running test. I found no "disk I/O error" in any gate log still on disk, so I cannot say more about it; the same teardown explanation fits (the journal's directory removed under it). The real, product-side gaps found while looking are the three commits above.
- inferd `settings_module::a_set_by...` and `cloud::the_picker...`: a D-Bus call that did not return in the rig's 60 s (`TimedOut` after 120 s in total) while accountd-sized tests around it ran 250 s: the environment (swap thrash). Nothing in inferd waits on a fixed short deadline in that path; its probe timeout (3 s in `main.rs`, 1 s in `hosts.rs`) is the one candidate left (see Left).

**Tests.** One `porter_fake::Deadline` (by the clock, `GENEROUS` = 120 s, "never happened: <what> (waited N s of 120 s)"), used by every counted wait in accountd, syncd and inferd `tests/it` (the common `eventually` of each, `serving()`, the name-release loops, the sign-in waits, the start tests) and inferd's `DEADLINE`. A passing wait still returns at once. 120 s because the failing tests ran 120 to 320 s in the logs; 60 s would have failed them.

**Left.** inferd probe timeouts (`PROBE_TIMEOUT` 3 s main.rs, 1 s hosts.rs): a probe that times out reads as Down until the next probe; I did not check for hysteresis. accountd test helpers `heard(.., 800 ms)` and several `Duration::from_secs(5)` waits for signals (agent_launcher, settings, agent_login, process_credential, places, agents) are still short positive waits. porter-client has no short fixed timeout on Linux (START_WAIT is the app's default for spawning an agent; the Windows pipe busy wait is 2 s). A before/after reproduction in a CPU-limited scope could not be completed: at load 115 to 140 the scope got under 1 s of CPU per 2 minutes, even the test listing did not finish.

## Lane choice-scores (how likely each option of a Choice was, for docket's decision-model trial; base `8ac5d6f`, stoker `916da297`, 2026-10-09)

**What it is.** An app that asks a `Choice` of two or more options can also ask, from the same call, how likely each option was. The answer is the model's own first-token probabilities, renormalised over the declared options: a hint for a shadow check, not a calibrated probability. It is off unless the request turns it on, it is only ever asked of and answered for a `Choice`, and it can never fail the call.

**The types** (porter-infer, all re-exported at the crate root; `Permille` is `porter_core::Permille(pub u32)`, `Count` is `porter_core::Count(pub u32)`):

```rust
pub struct ScoreOptions { pub top_k: Count }            // Default: Count(20) = MOST_CANDIDATES
pub const MOST_CANDIDATES: u32 = 20;
pub struct OptionScore { pub option: String, pub share: Permille }
pub struct OptionScores(/* private Vec<OptionScore> */); // serde: a JSON list of OptionScore
impl OptionScores {
    pub fn new(Vec<OptionScore>) -> Result<Self, ScoresError>; // two or more, each <= 1000, sum exactly 1000
    pub fn as_slice(&self) -> &[OptionScore];
    pub fn share_of(&self, option: &str) -> Option<Permille>;
}
pub enum ScoresError { TooFew, OverAWhole, NotAWhole(u32) }
// ChatControl gains:   pub scores: Knob<ScoreOptions>,       // Knob::Off by default
// ChatReply gains:     pub scores: Option<OptionScores>,     // None unless asked and the engine could tell
// Knob<T> gains:       pub fn is_off(&self) -> bool, and impl Default (Off)
```

`OptionScores` is checked on the way in too (`#[serde(try_from = "Vec<OptionScore>")]`): a frame whose shares do not add up to 1000 does not read, so a value that exists is a whole. The spelling of the permille is the plain number. "A newtype over 0..=1000": porter already has the `Permille` newtype (a `u32`, used for temperatures and thresholds, so unbounded); it is reused and the bound is `OptionScores`' (each share at most 1000, the sum exactly 1000), not a second type.

**Wire form** (Inference1's session frames are JSON on the returned fd; the bus XML is untouched):

- Request: `control.scores` is absent when `Off` (`skip_serializing_if`, `default`), and `{"kind":"set","v":{"top_k":20}}` when on. A daemon that has not heard of the field ignores it (serde ignores unknown keys); a client that does not write it gets `Off`.
- Reply: `scores` is absent when `None` (an older client reads the reply unchanged), and otherwise `[{"option":"allow","share":778},{"option":"deny","share":222}]`, in the declared order.
- Frozen-interface rule: the change is additive in serde (a defaulted, skipped-when-empty field in each of two structs), no variant, no method, no bus member and no existing field changed; `dbus/org.quire.Inference1.xml` and `porter-dbus` `inference1_xml_unchanged_members` are unchanged (the reply never was in the XML). **VocabVersion stays 11**: neither type is stored anywhere (no registry, no settings, no audit row carries a `ChatControl` or `ChatReply`).
- **Consumer break:** `ChatControl` and `ChatReply` are plain structs with public fields, so every struct literal of either needs the new field (`scores: Knob::Off` / `scores: None`). This lane edited those of this repo (inferd, porter-bridge, porter-client, porter-fake, porter-rig and their tests).

**Rounding.** Largest remainder over the renormalised masses, ties to the earlier declared option, so the shares are in the declared order and add up to exactly 1000 (three equal options: 334, 333, 333). stoker's `FirstTokenLogprobs::option_permille` does this and is used as it is; it did not fall short of the brief's rules. porter-bridge (`scores.rs`) is the one place the two `Permille` types (stoker's `u16`, porter's `u32`) meet; it also rebuilds the result through `OptionScores::new`, which checks the sum a second time.

**The rules, as stoker states them and the tests hold them** (`porter-bridge` `scores::tests`):

- A token counts for the option whose text starts with it (no tokenizer here). A token with mass that starts two options (`Al` for `Allow` and `Always`) makes the answer `None`: first-token scores cannot tell them apart, and stoker carries no whole-sequence log-probabilities to fall back on, so there is no fallback. One consequence: a rare token that happens to be a prefix of two options (`n` for `no` and `n/a`) also makes it `None`, even when the likely token is `no`. Strict on purpose; if docket sees many `None`s with such options, give the options distinct first letters or ask stoker for a whole-sequence variant.
- An option missing from the top-k has mass 0. If none of the options is present, `None`. Fewer than two options, `None`.
- A NaN from the engine never becomes a `Logprob` (stoker drops the entry); `-inf` is `Logprob::NEVER`, mass 0.
- The leading-space and case variants of a token (` allow`, `Allow` for `allow`) count for nobody. If an engine spells its first tokens that way, the answer is `None`, not wrong; this is unchecked against a recorded llama-server fixture (stoker says the same).

**What goes to the engine** (`porter_bridge::choice_scores`, called by `chat_turn_for`): `TurnRequest.choice_scores = ChoiceScores::FirstToken { top_k }` with `top_k` held to 1 through 20, only when `control.scores` is set and the shape is a `Choice` of two or more options; otherwise `Off`. vLLM and llama-server get `logprobs: true, top_logprobs: k` (stoker); hosted providers get nothing and a `Choice` is not served there at all today (stoker's codec refuses `OutputShape::Choice` for a hosted flavor), so scores on a hosted route are `None` when the Choice is not refused first. This build speaks no Anthropic wire (`cloud::models::WIRES` is the OpenAI-compatible one; Claude goes through OpenRouter, which stoker sends no `logprobs`), so "an Anthropic-shaped fake" is exercised as what it gives, a turn end with no first-token list (`reply::tests::a_turn_that_asked_and_got_nothing_usable_has_the_same_reply_without_scores`), not as a wire.

**inferd.** The two paths a chat turn takes both carry it. Checked (a `Choice` the engine constrains: `structured::run` keeps the first-token list of the attempt whose reply passed, never of a repaired attempt) and unchecked (`Gathered::for_turn(turn)` remembers the options). A turn that asked and got nothing usable gets the same reply with `scores: None` and one line in the journal (`inferd: choice scores: none: <reason>`; the daemon has no logger, so this is `eprintln!` like the rest of it). porter-client's in-process engines (`engines` feature) do the same with no line (a library does not print).

**inferd's OpenAI-compatible front.** It could not express a `Choice` at all before (`response_format` is text or `json_schema`), so there was nothing for `logprobs` to apply to. It now reads vLLM's `guided_choice` (or `structured_outputs.choice`), a non-empty list of strings, as `ReplyShape::Choice` (a list of anything else is refused as an unmapped feature, not ignored). With `logprobs: true` the reply carries `choices[0].logprobs`: `{"content":[{"token":"<the chosen option>","logprob":ln(share/1000),"bytes":[...],"top_logprobs":[{"token","logprob","bytes"}, ...]}],"refusal":null}`, the options with a share, likeliest first, at most `top_logprobs` of them (0 to 20, default 0); a share of 0 is `-9999.0` as OpenAI writes an unlikely token. The numbers are the options' shares as natural logarithms (rounded to thousandths, renormalised over the declared options), not the engine's token probabilities. When the engine gave none, `logprobs` is `null`. Streaming: the same object in a chunk of its own with an empty delta, just before the chunk with the finish reason (the checked reply arrives whole). **Not a Choice** (no `guided_choice`): `logprobs` and `top_logprobs` are dropped without a word as before, and the reply has no `logprobs` key. The Anthropic front is unchanged. An account route (forwarding to a company) forwards the body as it always did.

**Tests.** porter-infer: `scores::tests::*`, `round_trip::{a_reply_without_scores_writes_no_key_and_a_frame_from_before_them_reads, the_scores_of_a_reply_are_a_list_of_options_with_their_shares, a_reply_whose_shares_are_not_a_whole_does_not_read, the_score_knob_is_not_written_when_off_and_reads_when_absent}`. porter-bridge: `scores::tests::{the_shares_follow_the_declared_order_and_add_up_to_a_thousand, rounding_goes_to_the_largest_remainder_so_the_sum_is_exact, a_tie_between_two_options_splits_evenly, options_that_share_a_first_token_cannot_be_told_apart, an_option_missing_from_the_top_k_counts_as_nothing, when_no_option_is_in_the_top_k_there_are_no_scores, a_nan_is_dropped_and_minus_infinity_is_no_mass, an_engine_that_reported_nothing_is_told_apart_from_one_that_reported_nonsense, a_single_option_has_nothing_to_be_compared_with, only_a_choice_of_two_or_more_with_the_knob_set_asks_the_engine, the_number_of_candidates_asked_for_is_held_to_what_the_engines_take}`, `reply::tests::{a_turn_that_asked_gets_the_shares_of_its_options_on_the_reply, a_turn_that_asked_and_got_nothing_usable_has_the_same_reply_without_scores, a_turn_that_did_not_ask_never_gets_scores_whatever_the_engine_sent}`, `request::tests::a_choice_that_asks_for_scores_asks_the_engine_for_its_first_tokens_and_nothing_else_does`. Bus (jailed gate), `inferd` `tests/it/scores.rs`: `a_choice_that_asks_gets_each_options_share_and_the_engine_was_asked_for_first_tokens`, `the_same_choice_without_the_knob_asks_for_nothing_and_has_no_scores`, `an_engine_without_log_probabilities_gives_the_same_reply_without_scores`, `a_garbled_log_probability_answer_does_not_fail_the_turn`, `options_that_share_a_first_token_give_no_scores_and_the_same_reply`, `a_reply_that_is_not_a_choice_never_carries_scores`, `the_openai_front_returns_a_choices_shares_as_logprobs_when_asked_and_not_otherwise`, `the_openai_front_says_null_logprobs_when_the_engine_gave_none`; porter-client `dbus_open::a_choice_that_asks_for_scores_crosses_the_bus_and_its_scores_come_back_typed`. Each fails without its change (the knob and the field do not exist; an engine without logprobs is the case "never fails": a version that returned an error for it would fail `an_engine_without_log_probabilities_gives_the_same_reply_without_scores`).

**Stoker.** Pinned at `916da297` (FINDINGS "First-token log-probabilities for a Choice"). The re-pin needed one hand-edited lock line: `model-openai-compat` gained `log` as a dependency; `cargo metadata --locked` is clean. No `Quirks` literal in this repo. Unchecked: llama-server against a recorded fixture (stoker's note).

## Lane tailnet-account (Tailscale as an account kind; "Your computers" through Tailscale, base `8ac5d6f`, 2026-10-09)

Tailscale joins the account system. The person adds a Tailscale account the way they add any other; its state follows Tailscale; and accountd lists the person's other computers on `org.quire.Tailnet1`, for the shell, Settings and temor (`~/rs-wt/accounts/tailnet-temor.md`). temor and docket never ask Tailscale themselves.

**What was read.** Tailscale v1.80.0 (BSD-3-Clause), the source only, for field names: `ipn/ipnstate/ipnstate.go` (`Status`, `PeerStatus`, `TailnetStatus`), `tailcfg/tailcfg.go` (`Node`, `UserProfile`, `Hostinfo`), `client/tailscale/apitype/apitype.go` (`WhoIsResponse`, `LocalAPIHost = "local-tailscaled.sock"`), `ipn/backend.go` (`State`, `NotifyWatchOpt`, `Notify`) and `ipn/localapi/localapi.go` (the handlers, the `Host` check, the stream). No Tailscale or Go code is linked or copied; the dependency added is hyper (already in the workspace).

**porter-tailscale (new crate; `Cargo.toml` feature `io` off by default).** Pure: `Status::parse`, `WhoIs::parse`, `Notice::parse`, `Backend`, `Standing { Ready, SignedOut, Down, Changing }`, `Node`, `User`, `UserId`, `Tailnet`, `Status::{label, machines, me_user, user}`, `Node::{name, owner, machine}`, `WhoIs::owner`, `TailscaleError`. With `io`: `LocalApi::{new(socket), system(), with_program_dirs, with_timeout, status, whois(SocketAddr), start_login, watch}` and `Watch::next`. The HTTP is hyper's own client over a `UnixStream` (not porter-http, whose client reads whole bodies over TLS or loopback and cannot hold the watch stream's chunked body open; no new hand-written reader). The default socket is `/var/run/tailscale/tailscaled.sock` (`DEFAULT_SOCKET`), a default only: every test names its own. accountd takes another from `ACCOUNTD_TAILSCALE_SOCKET` (an absolute path; a relative one is ignored, as an XDG one is; `accountd::paths::Paths::tailscale_socket`), and every test that starts the accountd binary sets it to a socket in its scratch directory that nobody serves (`binary.rs`, `file_keys.rs`), since this computer runs a real tailscaled whose socket is open to every user. In-process tests leave `Options::tailnet` empty or give the fake's socket.
- `TailscaleError` (non-exhaustive; each variant's text is the plain sentence): `NotInstalled` "Tailscale isn't installed on this computer."; `NotRunning` "Tailscale isn't running on this computer."; `Refused` "This computer's Tailscale doesn't let your accounts ask it yet."; `SignedOut` "Tailscale is signed out."; `NoSuchPeer` "Tailscale does not know that computer."; `Malformed` "Tailscale gave an answer that couldn't be read."; `TimedOut` "Tailscale did not answer in time.". Socket missing and no `tailscaled` program in `PATH` or in the usual places is `NotInstalled`; missing socket with the program there, or nobody listening, is `NotRunning`; a refused connect or a `401`/`403` is `Refused`.
- Status: `BackendState` `Running` is `Ready`, `NeedsLogin` is `SignedOut`, `Stopped`/`NeedsMachineAuth`/`InUseOtherUser` are `Down`, `Starting`/`NoState`/unknown are `Changing`. The label of the account is `"{login} on {tailnet}"`, or the login alone when the network is named after it (a personal one).
- Owner of a computer: a tag makes it `tagged`; else shared in (`ShareeNode`, or `AltSharerUserID` set, or in `whois` `Sharer` set) or another user's makes it `shared`; else `mine`.
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `scripts/check-boundary.sh` list it: pure by default (no zbus, tokio, hyper), edges `porter-core` only; `porter-families` (feature `tailnet`) and `accountd` gain it.

**Account kind and group.** `AuthKind::OwnProgram` (slug `own_program`), new, "signed in by its own program". None of the existing kinds fits: `AgentLogin` is driven by a launcher that reports the state (`Peer.SetAgentState`), is in the Assistants group and is hidden from the add list while no launcher is registered; Tailscale has no launcher and accountd asks it directly. `sign_in_way` is `Outside` (the person signs in in the program). Provider file `providers/tailscale.toml`: id `tailscale`, label "Tailscale", `group = "internet"`, `[auth] kind = "own_program"`, `[discovery] kind = "fixed"`, `capability = []`: the parser now allows no capability rows for this kind only (its service is `Tailnet1`, not an `Accounts1` capability). Family: `porter-families` `tailnet` (`TailnetProvider`, `TailnetSession`, `TailnetSignIn`), a session mints no token (`Forbidden`), revoke is `Unsupported`, porter holds no secret.

**Adding it.** `AddAccount("tailscale")` (or the add list): Tailscale is asked who is signed in.
- Running and signed in: the review shows the label, and Confirm stores the account in state `ok`. A second add is `AlreadyAdded` (one Tailscale serves this computer).
- Signed out: Tailscale is asked to start its own sign-in (`POST login-interactive`), and its own page (`status.AuthURL`, `https://login.tailscale.com/...`) is the sheet's browser step; the sheet waits (a look every two seconds, five minutes at most, then `timed_out`) until Tailscale is signed in, then the review. If Tailscale gives no page, the sheet ends `signed_out`. porter takes no password, key or code.
- Signing in again (Settings' "Sign in" on the account) is the same conversation without the review.
- Faults are the sheet's closed words. `not_installed` (existing), and three new `SignInFault`s: `not_running`, `signed_out`, `not_allowed`. porter does not word them (the shell does): the sentences to use are Tailscale's above ("Tailscale isn't installed on this computer.", "Tailscale isn't running on this computer.", "Tailscale is signed out." with "Open the Tailscale app to sign in." beside it, "This computer's Tailscale doesn't let your accounts ask it yet."). `refusal_of` answers an app `Unavailable` for all of them.
- Help text (Settings rows): the account's "Sign in" row, shown while it needs one: "Opens Tailscale's own sign-in page. Your Tailscale password is never seen here."; the "Remove account" row for this kind: "Takes this account out of this computer's accounts. It does not sign Tailscale out." Removing the account makes no request that changes Tailscale (`removing_the_account_does_not_sign_tailscale_out`).

**State follows tailscaled: watch, with a look behind it, and why.** `watch-ipn-bus` is usable over the socket (HTTP/1.1, chunked, newline-delimited JSON, flushed per notice), asked with mask 282 (initial state 2, initial net map 8, no private keys 16, rate limit 256; without 16 the stream needs write access). accountd reads the stream only as a nudge and looks at `status` on each notice (the net map is large and its shape is Tailscale's to change). The stream does not replace a look: it needs a running tailscaled, and some versions do not announce a peer going offline. So accountd also looks every 30 seconds while the stream is open, and tries to open it again every 10 seconds while it is not. State: running and signed in `ok`; signed out `needs_login` (the state that means "sign in again", and Settings offers "Sign in"); turned off, not running, not installed, not letting porter ask `offline` (the account is kept); starting: unchanged. Timing is `TailnetWatch { api, backstop: 30 s, retry: 10 s, coalesce: 1 s }` (tests shorten it).

**Tailnet1 (`org.quire.Tailnet1` on accountd, `/org/quire/Tailnet1`; additive: a new interface and a new XML file, `dbus/org.quire.Tailnet1.xml`; no member of `Accounts1`, `Spaces1` or any other changed).**
- `Machines() -> a(sa{sv})`: one row per other computer, none for this one; the first part is the Tailscale node id (stable across renames and address changes). The dict: `node` s (the same id), `name` s (the first part of its network name), `dns` s (MagicDNS without the dot at the end: connect to this), `addresses` as, `os` s, `online` b, `last_seen` x (Unix seconds; absent while online and when unknown), `ssh` b (the computer advertises SSH host keys), `ssh_host_keys` as, `owner` s (`mine`, `shared` or `tagged`). Rows are in name order. Keys are `porter_dbus::MACHINE_KEY_*`; `machine_to_dbus` and `machine_from_dbus` are the codec (real `as` arrays, not lists of variants). `node` is in the dict too because temor's note lists it there.
- Empty list, not an error, with no Tailscale account in porter, with one that is not `ok`, or while Tailscale says it is not running and signed in. The account's own state says why.
- `Changed()`: no payload; a listener calls `Machines` again. A burst of changes is one signal (the first change opens a window of one second; when it ends accountd reads again and signals once if the list differs from what was last told), so at most one a second. Sent to each allowed connection that has called accountd (call `Machines` once, or `Tailnet::watch`, to be told), never to an app.
- Callers: the shell (`SheetHost`), Settings (`Settings`) and temor (new `CallerRole::Terminal`, `callers.toml` role `terminal`). Everyone else, and a sender accountd does not know, is `AccessDenied`. `Terminal` may do nothing else at accountd: every other method refuses it `AccessDenied` (`identify`). A new role was needed because a unit-bound row gives its role to that unit alone, and a role of `app` cannot be told from any app scope of the same name; the `Spaces1.Create` list and syncd's `Access` name it too (inferd's `from_shared` already maps every other role to `App`).
- `dist/callers.toml` row for temor, whose unit is confirmed: `app = "org.quire.Temor"`, `unit = "org.quire.Temor.service"` (`Type=dbus`, as detent's `org.quire.Settings.service`), `role = "terminal"`. The unit is checked against the manager's main process (the MainPID rule) like every unit row.

**Rust types.**
- porter-core (re-exported by porter-client): `NodeId` (checked newtype, 1 to 64 of letters, digits, `-`, `_`; serde string), `MachineOwner { Mine, Shared, Tagged }` (`slug()`, `from_slug()`, `ALL`), `Machine { node: NodeId, name: String, dns: String, addresses: Vec<IpAddr>, os: String, online: bool, last_seen: Option<UnixSeconds>, ssh: bool, ssh_host_keys: Vec<String>, owner: MachineOwner }`.
- porter-client (feature `dbus`): `DbusTransport::tailnet() -> Result<Tailnet, TailnetError>`; `Tailnet::{connect, machines() -> Vec<Machine>, watch() -> MachineChanges}` (it subscribes, then reads once, so accountd counts the connection); `MachineChanges` is a `BusStream<Item = ()>`; `TailnetError { Denied(String), Unreachable, Malformed(String) }` (non-exhaustive). temor and Settings parse no dict.
- porter-dbus: `TailnetProxy`, `TailnetSkeleton`, `TailnetChanged`, `TailnetChangedStream`, `Bus::Tailnet`, `TAILNET_PATH`.
- accountd: `TailnetWatch` (public: `Options::tailnet: Option<TailnetWatch>`, `TailnetWatch::{new(LocalApi), system()}`); `providers::FamilyIo` gains `tailscale: LocalApi` and `with_tailscale`.

**Vocabulary.** `VocabVersion` 11 to 12: `AuthKind::OwnProgram`, `SignInFault::{NotRunning, SignedOut, NotAllowed}`, `Machine`, `MachineOwner`, `NodeId`, and the `terminal` caller role. Migration 11 to 12 moves only the version (a document written at 11 has none of them); frozen document `AT_TWELVE` (an `own_program` account in `needs_login`) in `store/tests.rs`; `Tailnet1`'s XML and introspection test. Consumers with an exhaustive match need arms: `AuthKind` (`OwnProgram`), `SignInFault` (the three), `CallerRole` (`Terminal`), `FamilyProvider`/`FamilySession`/`FamilySignIn` (`Tailnet`, behind the `tailnet` feature) and `ProviderRow` consumers of the shipped list (a 24th file).

**Residuals.** Same-uid trust: the socket answers any user it is open to, and porter reads only what Tailscale gives that user. The `Terminal` row matches only the process that is `org.quire.Temor.service`'s main process, so a temor run any other way (a scope of the same name) is refused. `node` and `NodeId` are Tailscale's stable id (`ID` in status, `StableID` in whois), never the numeric node id or the node key, so a renamed computer keeps it (test `a_renamed_computer_keeps_its_node_id`). The account's label is the one read when it was added; a different login on the same computer is not renamed (the person removes and adds). `Down` covers a Tailscale that waits for an administrator (`NeedsMachineAuth`); the sentence for that is not separate. The fake LocalAPI follows v1.80.0; a later version that renames a field reads as an empty list, not an error, by design (`Status::parse` takes what it finds).

**Tests.** porter-tailscale units: `time::tests::a_time_reads_to_unix_seconds`, `status::tests::*` (states, peers, last seen, label, signed out, bad ids, malformed), `whois::tests::*`, `notice::tests::*`, `error::tests::every_sentence_is_plain_and_ends_with_a_full_stop`; integration (fake LocalAPI on a scratch socket) `status::{a_running_tailscale_lists_the_other_computers, a_signed_out_tailscale_answers_and_gives_its_page_only_after_a_sign_in_is_asked, a_tailscale_in_the_middle_of_changing_says_so, the_client_sends_the_host_tailscale_expects_and_nothing_to_alarm_it}`, `whois::*`, `watch::*`, `errors::{no_socket_and_no_program_is_not_installed, no_socket_but_the_program_is_there_is_not_running, a_socket_nobody_listens_on_is_not_running, a_daemon_that_answers_forbidden_is_refused_for_every_question, a_user_who_may_read_but_not_sign_in_is_refused_the_sign_in_only, a_socket_that_is_not_tailscale_is_malformed, a_daemon_that_never_answers_times_out}`. porter-core: `the_tailscale_values_keep_their_slugs`, the frozen `a_document_frozen_at_each_vocabulary_since_the_first_stored_one_reads` (12), `what_a_version_added_reads_as_what_it_was`, `tailnet::tests::*`. porter-provider: `the_tailscale_file_holds_no_credential_and_offers_apps_no_capability`, the shipped-file tables. porter-families: `tailnet::sign_in::tests::each_way_tailscale_cannot_be_asked_has_its_own_word`. porter-dbus: `the_tailnet_object_declares_machines_and_changed_and_nothing_else`, `checked_in_introspection_matches_the_interfaces` (Tailnet), `machines::tests::*`. Bus (jailed gate), accountd `tests/it/tailnet.rs`: `adding_tailscale_when_it_is_running_files_an_account_that_holds_nothing`, `adding_tailscale_when_it_is_signed_out_hands_over_its_own_page_and_waits`, `adding_tailscale_says_in_closed_words_why_it_could_not`, `adding_tailscale_twice_is_the_same_account`, `the_account_follows_tailscale_while_it_is_added`, `a_change_the_watch_did_not_announce_is_found_by_the_look_behind_it`, `removing_the_account_does_not_sign_tailscale_out`, `machines_lists_every_kind_of_computer_and_not_this_one`, `machines_is_empty_with_no_account_and_with_one_that_is_not_working`, `changed_fires_once_for_a_burst_and_again_for_the_next_change`, `only_the_shell_settings_and_the_terminal_may_read_the_computers`, `the_terminal_reads_the_computers_and_is_refused_everything_else`, `a_renamed_computer_keeps_its_node_id` (also porter-tailscale `status::a_renamed_computer_keeps_its_stable_node_id`); porter-client `dbus_tailnet::{the_terminal_reads_typed_machines_and_hears_of_changes, an_app_and_a_stranger_are_refused_with_the_reason_read_back}`.

**For temor and detent (the exact API).** `org.quire.Tailnet1` at `/org/quire/Tailnet1` on `org.quire.Accounts1`: `Machines() -> a(sa{sv})` and `Changed()` as above; in Rust `DbusTransport::over(connection).tailnet().await?` then `machines()` and `watch()`. temor tells account its unit name (the placeholder `org.quire.Temor.service`). detent shows the Tailscale account like any other (group "Internet Accounts", label "ada@example.org on example.org", `needs_login` offers "Sign in"); the new `SignInFault`s are worded by the shell as above.

**Owner's steps to try it on the real tailnet.** (1) On a computer with Tailscale installed and signed in, start accountd from this build as usual (`systemctl --user restart accountd.service`, or `accountd` in a terminal). (2) Open Settings, Accounts, add an account, pick Tailscale: it should say the login and network and add after one click. If it says "doesn't let your accounts ask it yet", run `sudo tailscale set --operator=$USER` once (Tailscale's own step) and try again. (3) With the account added, `busctl --user call org.quire.Accounts1 /org/quire/Tailnet1 org.quire.Tailnet1 Machines` should list the other computers; it is refused for any caller but the shell, Settings and temor, so run it from the Settings unit or use a throwaway row in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/porter/callers.toml` giving your terminal's scope the role `terminal`. (4) Turn Tailscale off (`tailscale down`): the account goes "Offline" within seconds; `tailscale up`: it works again. (5) `tailscale logout`: the account says it needs a sign in and Settings shows "Sign in", which opens Tailscale's own page. (6) Remove the account in Settings: Tailscale stays signed in (`tailscale status`). Nothing in these steps types a Tailscale password into porter.

## Lane fix-loadflake-2 (what Part 1 left; base `b4e0885`, 2026-10-10)

**Product (one commit).** inferd's probes. Two kinds, two answers.
- The runtimes the person runs (Ollama, llama.cpp, LM Studio; `watch.rs`): a look in which an online runtime did not answer marked it `Offline` at once, reported `offline` to accountd, and its models were `Unavailable` for the next ten seconds at least. One request through the 3 s `PROBE_TIMEOUT` that a loaded computer answered late did that. Now a `Down` change is held back when some request of that look TIMED OUT (not when the connection was refused: nothing listening is a stop at once), the next look comes in 2 s, and the runtime goes offline only on the third such look in a row (`watch::confirm`, `MISSES_TO_DOWN` = 3). The models stay served meanwhile; a runtime that really stopped is down after three looks (a few seconds), one that only answers late is never down. `PROBE_TIMEOUT` is now 10 s. Tests: `watch::tests::{a_runtime_lost_to_timeouts_is_down_only_on_the_third_look_in_a_row, a_slow_look_does_not_end_a_runtimes_models_but_a_stop_still_does}` (the second wraps the client in one that times out on demand). Without the fix the second fails at its first look after the switch (the model is `Unavailable`, accountd was told `offline`).
- The engines inferd starts itself (`hosts.rs`, `HealthProbe`, 1 s): not a routing decision. stoker's supervisor asks again every 500 ms and reads `Down` as "not ready yet" until the engine's start timeout (180 s); only a `Ready` answer changes anything, and a `Ready` engine is not probed again. So a slow probe cost nothing except when EVERY answer takes more than a second, which fails the start at the timeout. The timeout is now 10 s. Test: `hosts::tests::an_engine_that_answers_health_after_a_second_and_a_half_is_ready` (a real 1.5 s delay; it was `Down`).
- Everything else I read in inferd and porter-client for fixed short deadlines: none on a path a person waits on (porter-client's Linux paths have no short fixed timeout; `START_WAIT` and the Windows pipe wait were noted in Part 1).

**Tests.**
- accountd: `heard(.., 800 ms)` collected signals for a fixed window; the waits for a signal that SHOULD come are now `common::heard_names(stream, &[expected..])`, which waits by the clock (`Deadline`, 120 s) until every expected name has come and then collects extras for 300 ms, so "once each" and "only this one" still hold. The short windows that remain (`heard(.., 300 ms)`, `told(300 ms)`, `hears_nothing`) prove an absence and say so in a comment: a slow machine can only make them pass. The `Duration::from_secs(5)` waits in agent_launcher, settings, agent_login, process_credential are `porter_fake::GENEROUS`; inferd `tests/it` places and agents (the 5 s waits and two counted loops) and `hosted/members.rs` (two counted loops Part 1 missed) likewise.
- The counted-sleep helpers of porter-client (`eventually`, the token wait, the spawn test's 15 s wait for the agent), porter-rig (`eventually`, the secrets name wait, `RunningServer::stop`'s 3 s port wait, the client test's 60 s line waits), porter-families (the listener-closed wait), inferd's `tests/*.rs` (engine_group, shutdown, attached, engine_start: `wait_for`, `gone_soon`, `until`, the lab and `want` waits) and the unit tests of inferd and syncd (hosts `pid_in`/`gone_soon`, settings reload, driver_wake, photos registration) are `Deadline` by the clock. `engine_start`'s "at once" checks (`took < 3 s`, `< 1 s`) became `< 25 s` (`FAST`: well under the 30 s and 180 s a waiting request would take), and the fake engines' self-exit is 360 s, not 60 s, so a long wait does not outlive its child. Not changed on purpose: step-count loops (`drive`, a sign-in's steps), `paused`-clock tests, and the sleeps that are a minimum (the 3.1 s pause in engine_start, the 900 ms idle in attached).

**Standing load check.** `.config/nextest.toml` has a profile `crowded`: `test-threads = 128`, four times the 32 CPUs of this machine (nextest takes an integer or `num-cpus` only, no multiplier; `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS=<4 x nproc>` overrides it). Run as the gate runs the archive, with `--profile crowded`. Numbers, jailed, 2425 tests:
- On the Part 1 tip `b4e0885` (`--test-threads 128`, base archive): 2422 run, 2422 passed, 45.0 s; load average at the start 98 (other lanes' builds).
- On this lane's tip (profile `crowded`): 2425 run, 2425 passed, 45.5 s; load average at the start 38.
- Nothing failed, so there was nothing for the profile to find. Run 128 at once, the tests are mostly waiting, not computing, so the contention is small next to a host at load 100 with swap full (the failing runs of 2026-10-09). I did not run a larger multiple: swap was full and the machine is shared. What the profile does catch: a wait that is a count of sleeps, or a bus name whose owner needs a turn that never comes.
- Proposal, not done (the gate script is not mine to change): the gate does not run `crowded` (it adds 45 s and found nothing); a nightly job should, and a lane that touches a wait, a timeout or a start-up order should run it once by hand: `gate-lane-jailed.sh`'s nextest line with `--profile crowded`.

**Left.** Not reproduced under a CPU-limited scope (as in Part 1); the owner's rule rests on reading the code and the product tests above. `BOUND` (30 s) in accountd's `agent_launcher` test is the launcher's own answer bound as the test configures it, not a test wait; I left it.

## Lane tailnet-serve (inferd across the tailnet; base `a7c11d2`, rebased on `c3a7b4c`, 2026-10-10)

A computer lends the models that run on it to the person's other computers over their Tailscale network, and a computer calls those of another. The logic is a library, `porter-tailnet` (pure by default, sockets behind `io`); inferd's `tailnet` module is the wiring. Built on `porter-tailscale` (`status`, `whois`, `watch`) and `Tailnet1`; no new HTTP reader (the listener's requests are read by `agent::http`, the agent endpoint's reader, and answered by `agent::local::run`; the hello is read by hyper's client).

**Serving.** `ai.tailnet.serve` (`off` | `on`, default off; the Intelligence page row "Let my other computers use this computer's models", `dist/inferd.settings.toml`; `Settings::tailnet_serve`). While off nothing is listened on and Tailscale is not asked (`Live` is now a `watch` channel: `Engines::watch_settings`). While on, `porter_tailnet::lend` listens on this computer's Tailscale addresses (from `status`, followed by `Observer`: the watch stream as a nudge, a look every 30 s behind it, a new try every 10 s without a stream; a passing failure keeps what was known) and nowhere else: only `100.64.0.0/10` and `fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48` in a product (`Config::product()`, `is_network_address`), never an unspecified address even if a config allowed it. An address that goes loses its listener and its connections; a new one gets one; a bind that fails is retried with a pause that doubles from 1 s to 30 s for as long as it is wanted. **Port 26434**, `porter_tailnet::PORT`: above 1024 (no privilege), below 32768 where Linux hands out outgoing ports, none of the ports model servers use (11434, 1234, 8080, 8000), the same on every computer so a peer is found without being told; not checked against the live IANA registry.
- **Every connection** is judged before a byte is read (`porter_tailnet::judge`): (c) from one of this computer's own addresses, or Tailscale says it is this node: refused without asking Tailscale (a local program of another account reaching the listener through the network address would otherwise be "your computer": test `a_request_from_this_computers_own_address_is_refused`, library and bus); Tailscale does not know the address / cannot be asked / lists other addresses: refused; the person said no: refused; the person said yes: let in whoever it is; else a tagged server (b), a shared-in or another user's computer (a): refused; else the person's own computer not answered about: let through as "new". Refusals are answered `403`/`429`/`503` with a JSON `error.message` in the sentences below.
- **First connection.** The person is asked once per computer, when it first asks for a *model* (`/v1/models`, chat), not for its hello: `Welcome::admit` puts it on the waiting list (at most 16) and refuses it with "waiting" until answered. Approval is by Tailscale node id (`Guests`, `$XDG_STATE_HOME/quire/inferd/guests.toml`, `AtomicWrite::PRIVATE`, an unreadable file is moved aside to `.unusable`); the questions waiting are not kept across a restart. Only the person's own computers raise a question; another user's, a server or a shared computer is never asked about and is let in only by Settings' word ahead of time (`AnswerGuest` as Settings, for any computer `Tailnet1` lists). **Deviation from the brief ("accountd's sheet flow"):** `SheetView` is the account flow's views and accountd's host only draws those; a confirmation that belongs to no account would be a new view, a `VocabVersion` bump and a path through accountd for one question. fix-confirm/fix-notice's path for held deletions is a signal to the shell plus a method (`Sync1.NeedsConfirmation`, `Watch`, `ConfirmDiscard`), so this reuses that: inferd emits `GuestAsks(node, {name, since})`, the shell draws "Let <name> use this computer's models?" and answers with `AnswerGuest`; a shell that was not running reads `Guests()` (state `asking`). No answer, or no, refuses the connection in words.
- **What a peer may use**: `Engines::lendable()`: language models with locality on-device that can answer now (an engine inferd runs, a runtime the person runs here, an attached engine whose place is this device; attached ones are looked at first, and only those, since looking at the others would ask the computers that may be asking us). Never a cloud account, never an attached network machine or a tailnet computer, no forward onward; not a setting. Any other model named is `404` and nothing is run (`a_cloud_account_is_never_reached_from_a_peer_even_when_it_is_the_only_engine`, `a_computer_attached_over_the_network_is_never_lent_onward`: the provider and the lab machine saw no request).
- **Protocol**: OpenAI Chat Completions over HTTP, the one inferd speaks to its agents and to attached engines (so the caller needs nothing new), with the catalogue id as the model's name. `GET /hello` (any computer that passed; `{service, version, models:[{id,name}], needs_approval}`, and nothing else about the computer), `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions` (streamed or whole). Runs through `agent::local::run`, so the audit line (app `org.quire.Guest.<node>`, tokens, never content), the engine path and the floor are the agent endpoint's.
- **Limits** (the house's): 64 MiB request bodies and the 120 s head wait of the agent endpoint, plus 4 connections from one computer and 16 in all (`Limits`; over them `429` "busy"); a request that waits for an answer is refused at once, not held.

**Calling.** `Inference1.Candidates() -> a(sa{sv})` (Settings only): node id, then `name` s, `models` a(ss), `needs_approval` b (`CANDIDATE_KEY_*`); the person's own online computers that `Tailnet1.Machines` lists (so never while Tailscale is not an account: accountd answers empty; `PorterDaemon` may now read `Machines`, inferd's row in `callers.toml`) and whose port answers the hello, minus those already added. A look is made only inside this call, at most once a minute for each computer (an unanswering one is left alone 1, 2, 4, 8 minutes), each after Tailscale is asked who is at the address (`Dialer`: status, then whois of the address it is about to dial, then a connection out of this computer's own network address; the same for every relay connection, so a computer tagged after it was added is turned away at once: `bytes_go_both_ways_and_each_connection_asks_tailscale_again`). Probe wait 15 s (a look at another computer, retried later, not inferd's own work). **`Places()` is unchanged**: a candidate is not a place until added (docket would offer a place that cannot serve).
- `AddTailnetComputer(node s) -> s` (Settings only): adds the candidate to `computers.toml` as `[computers.<name>] label, node, models` (no address, no key), answers `computer:<name>`; the name is made from Tailscale's machine name, and `Places`/`follow_names` keep the label following it (the place id stays). Models are those the peer lends that the catalogue has and this computer does not run itself (a model this computer can launch is not borrowed: both would be `local/<id>`; a start-up clash keeps the local one and says so). `RemoveComputer` is unchanged (it also stops the relay). The engine is `Reach::Tailnet { node, socket }`, an attached engine reached at a relay socket `<runtime>/inferd/tailnet-<node>.sock` (`Relay`: one local path per computer, bytes both ways, a canned `503` with the reason when it cannot reach), `Place::MyNetwork` so **`Locality::LocalNetwork`**: the floor, `local_only`, `ai.attached.my_network` and the places rules apply as for any computer. Its catalogue entry may be launched or attached on the peer (`local_model` accepts both for this reach; wire flavor `LiteLlm`, name the catalogue id). The attached look waits 60 s at each stage for such a computer (not 3 s).
- Errors, `org.quire.Inference1.Error.Computer.<Name>`, new: `NotOnTailscale` "That is not one of your computers on Tailscale.", `NotAnswering` "That computer isn't lending its models right now. Check that it is on, and that it is set to let your other computers use its models.", `NotAsking` "That computer is not asking to use this computer's models.", `TooManyAsking` "Too many computers are asking at once. Answer some of them first."
- **Settings lists and revokes approvals** on `Inference1`: `Guests() -> a(sa{sv})` (Settings, shell): node id, `name`, `state` approved|denied|asking, `since` x (`GUEST_KEY_*`); `AnswerGuest(node s, answer s)` (`answer` is a word, `allow` or `deny`: `GUEST_ANSWER_ALLOW`, `GUEST_ANSWER_DENY`, in Rust `porter_tailnet::GuestAnswer { Allow, Deny }` with `slug`/`from_slug`; any other word is `InvalidArgs`; shell: only a computer that is asking; Settings: any computer on the network; a `deny` keeps it out and it is not asked again, so a yes is taken back by a `deny`; no bool parameter anywhere: `Guests::{answer, set}` take `GuestAnswer` and `Hello::new` takes `Approval { Needed, Given }`; `needs_approval` b and the bool in `Candidates` rows stay as row data, like `Places`' `ready`); `ForgetGuest(node s)` (Settings): back to a stranger, asked again next time; signals `GuestAsks(node, details)` and `GuestsChanged()` (broadcast; a name is not a secret and only the shell and Settings can answer).

**Frozen interfaces.** Additive on `org.quire.Inference1` (five methods, two signals; `dbus/org.quire.Inference1.xml`, skeleton, proxy and the introspection test in the same commit). No `VocabVersion` bump: nothing in porter-core's wire types changed (`NodeId` and `Machine` are tailnet-account's), and the stored forms are inferd's own files (`computers.toml` gains an optional `node`, `guests.toml` is new), as in the places lane. `AuditEntry` is unchanged.

**Plain words** (to the person who reads on the calling computer; "that computer" lends, "this" asks): "That computer won't answer a request that came from itself." / "That computer could not tell which of your computers is asking." / "That computer could not check who is asking." / "That computer won't lend its models to this one, which is set up as a server." / "That computer won't lend its models to this one, which belongs to someone else, unless it is allowed in Settings there." / "That computer was told not to let this one use its models. This can be changed in Settings there." / "That computer is asking its owner whether to let this one use its models. Say yes there, then try again." / "That computer is already asking about several computers. Answer those first." / "That computer could not keep the answer. Try again." / "That computer is busy with this one's other requests. Try again in a moment." / "That computer isn't lending its models." The dial says: "That computer is not on your Tailscale network any more." / "That computer is not one of your own." / "That computer is switched off or out of reach." / "That computer's place on the network now belongs to another computer." / "That computer did not answer." (words tests in each crate: no program name, "socket", "node", "tailnet", "address"). The sheet's question for the shell to word: "Let <name> use this computer's models?"

**Sandbox.** `dist/inferd.service` allows loopback only; lending and calling need the network's own addresses, so `dist/inferd-tailnet.conf` (opt-in drop-in like `inferd-cloud.conf`: `IPAddressAllow=100.64.0.0/10 fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48`, not the internet) goes in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/systemd/user/inferd.service.d/tailnet.conf` (since lane install-tailnet: `scripts/install.sh` installs it as data at `$prefix/share/porter/inferd-tailnet.conf` and the Settings switch installs the drop-in, with the person's consent, through `porter_client::TailnetLending`; the script never writes the live drop-in). Without it the setting is on and nobody can connect (dist test `the_tailnet_drop_in_opens_the_networks_own_addresses_and_nothing_else`). **For Whopper/sill:** subscribe to `GuestAsks` on `org.quire.Inference1`, read `Guests()` at start; the question is "Let <name> use this computer's models?" with Yes (`AnswerGuest(node, "allow")`) and No (`"deny"`).

**For detent and docket (Rust).** `porter_dbus::InferenceProxy::{candidates, add_tailnet_computer, guests, answer_guest, forget_guest, receive_guest_asks, receive_guests_changed}`; keys `CANDIDATE_KEY_{NAME,MODELS,NEEDS_APPROVAL}`, `GUEST_KEY_{NAME,STATE,SINCE}`; errors `COMPUTER_ERROR_PREFIX` + `NotOnTailscale|NotAnswering|NotAsking|TooManyAsking`. porter-client has no wrappers (Settings calls the proxy, as for `AddComputer`). docket needs nothing new: a tailnet computer is `computer:<name>` in `Places`, `kind = own_computer`.

**Residuals.** The brief's "Places gains candidate computers" is read as the separate `Candidates` list. A computer's models are those it lent when it was added (one it adds later is not borrowed until it is added again; one it stops lending shows as not ready). Two machines with the same name cannot both be added (`AlreadyThere`). A model this computer can itself launch is not borrowed. Structured output beyond a JSON schema is whatever the peer's OpenAI front maps. The shell must be running to draw the question; until then the computer is told to wait. Same-uid trust as everywhere: any program of the person's can call the unix socket of Tailscale and the relay sockets (mode 0700 directory). Not verified against the real tailscaled (whois of a remote peer's address with a port).

**Owner's steps on the real tailnet.** (1) On two computers of yours with Tailscale signed in and added as an account in Settings, install `dist/inferd-tailnet.conf` as above on both, `systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user restart inferd.service`; Tailscale's socket must be open to you (`sudo tailscale set --operator=$USER`). (2) On the computer with the models, turn on "Let my other computers use this computer's models". (3) On the other, in Settings, look for computers (`busctl --user call org.quire.Inference1 /org/quire/Inference1 org.quire.Inference1 Candidates` from the Settings unit, or Settings' own list) and add the first. (4) The first time it asks for a model, the lending computer shows the question (or `busctl --user call ... Guests` shows `asking`); answer yes. (5) The assistant on the second computer can now be placed at `computer:<name>`. (6) Say no / forget it in Settings and watch it refused. (7) `tailscale down` on the lending computer: its listener goes; `tailscale up`: it returns. Nothing here types a Tailscale password into porter.

**Tests.** porter-tailnet (57 + 1): `guests::tests::*`, `judge::tests::*` (every refusal by table, the own-address rule, by-id answers), `refusal::tests::*`, `hello::tests::*`, `dial::tests::*`, `lend::*` (off by default, follows addresses, never every address, retry, away and back, refusals with words, own address, limits, drop closes), `dial::*`, `relay::*`, `greet::*`, `observe::*`. inferd bus (jail), `tests/it/tailnet.rs`: `lending_is_off_by_default_nothing_listens_and_tailscale_is_not_even_asked`, `a_new_computer_is_asked_about_once_and_after_a_yes_its_models_answer` (approval granted, Candidates, AddTailnetComputer, chat on the lending computer's engine), `a_no_keeps_a_computer_out_and_it_is_not_asked_again` (denied), `a_yes_can_be_taken_back_and_forgotten_so_the_computer_is_asked_again` (revoked), `another_user_a_server_and_a_shared_computer_are_refused_unless_the_person_allows_it`, `a_request_from_pis_own_address_is_refused_and_raises_no_question`, `a_cloud_account_is_never_reached_from_a_peer_even_when_it_is_the_only_engine`, `a_computer_attached_over_the_network_is_never_lent_onward`, `the_listener_follows_the_address_tailscale_gives_the_computer`, `candidates_are_looked_at_only_when_read_at_most_once_a_minute_and_only_with_an_account`, `adding_a_computer_that_is_not_there_or_twice_is_refused_in_plain_words`, `the_name_follows_tailscale_and_the_place_stays`, `only_settings_may_add_or_look_and_only_settings_and_the_shell_may_answer`; units `attached::computers::tests::*` (tailnet), `dist::*`; accountd `the_ai_broker_reads_the_computers_to_find_the_ones_that_lend_its_models`.

## Lane layers-fs (the file writer out of porter-core; base `55d1ade`, 2026-10-10)

**What moved.** `porter_core::atomic` (`atomic.rs` and its `atomic/tests.rs`, unchanged byte for byte: `AtomicWrite`, `AtomicFile`, `Parents`, `Visibility`, `staging_beside`, the thread-local sync counter for the tests) is now the crate `porter-fs`, module `porter_fs::atomic`. The crate has no dependencies (std only; the module uses no thiserror and no porter-core type, so neither is listed). `porter-core` loses `pub mod atomic`, its lib doc says it is pure (no runtime, no file system, no I/O), and its check-boundary row now also greps its source for `std::fs`, `std::process` and `std::env`.

**Who imports it now** (all switched to `use porter_fs::atomic::...`, each with `porter-fs = { workspace = true }` beside its `porter-core` line; the root `[workspace.dependencies]` gains `porter-fs = { path = "crates/porter-fs" }`): accountd (`store.rs`, `spaces.rs`: `AtomicFile`), inferd (`attached/computers.rs`, `cloud/spend.rs`, `settings/file.rs`: `AtomicWrite`), porter-secrets (`file.rs`), porter-tailnet (`guests.rs`: `AtomicWrite`), syncd (`datasets/photos/cas.rs`, `datasets/pim/vdir.rs`). Nothing in porter re-exports the old path: porter-core cannot, since porter-fs is not one of its dependencies and the other way would be a cycle.

**Consumers outside porter** (`git grep -n "porter_core::atomic"`, rc 1 = no match): docket, almanac, cua, mailo, sill, detent and casement at `origin/master`, and anyview at `origin/main`: zero hits in all eight. Nothing outside porter imports `porter_core::atomic`, so no consumer breaks.

**Boundary.** porter-fs reaches none of the effect crates; porter-core reaches none (unchanged). The EDGES table has a `porter-fs:` row (no deps) and the five users list it. `Cargo.lock` gains only the `porter-fs` entry and the new `porter-fs` dependency lines.

**Tests.** `cargo nextest run -p porter-fs -p porter-core`: 175 of 175 pass (the moved tests keep their names under `porter-fs atomic::tests::*`). `cargo check --workspace --all-features --tests`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check` and `scripts/check-boundary.sh` all pass.

## Lane layers-clock (the time seam in porter-core; base `eb29bd3`, 2026-10-10)

**What moved.** `porter_core::clock` (pure, std time only): the `Clock` trait (`fn now(&self) -> UnixSeconds`, `Send + Sync`, the same method as every copy), `SystemClock` (a unit struct, `Debug, Clone, Copy, Default`), `FixedClock(pub UnixSeconds)` with `new` and `set(&mut self, _)`, and `impl Clock for Arc<T>` (the blanket syncd had; it can only live in porter-core, since `Arc` is foreign to every other crate). `check-boundary.sh` checks `porter-core/src/clock.rs` for std fs, io, env, process and net names, like `identity.rs`.

| Old copy | Now | Old path |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `porter_service::Clock` (`porter-service/src/clock.rs`) | `pub use porter_core::clock::Clock` | kept: `porter_service::Clock` |
| syncd `clock.rs` (`Clock`, `SystemClock`, the `Arc` blanket) | `pub use` of `Clock, SystemClock`; the `Arc` impl is gone from syncd (core has it) | kept: `syncd::clock::{Clock, SystemClock}` |
| syncd `ManualClock` | stays in syncd (see below) | kept |
| inferd `clock.rs` (`Clock`, `SystemClock`, `FixedClock`) | `pub use` of all three | kept: `inferd::clock::*` |
| porter-fake `FixedClock` | `pub use porter_core::clock::FixedClock` | kept: `porter_fake::FixedClock` |
| accountd `clock.rs` (`pub(crate) SystemClock`) | `pub(crate) use porter_core::clock::SystemClock` | kept (crate-private) |
| accountd `launchers.rs` (private `SystemClock`, the `LoginTiming` fallback) | `use porter_core::clock::SystemClock`; the struct is deleted | not a public path |
| storage_webdav `Clock` (a struct: `Clock::fixed`, `Clock::system`, `now`, Debug) | thin adapter: `Clock(Arc<dyn porter_core::clock::Clock>)` with the same three functions | kept: `storage_webdav::Clock` (and the `storage_graph` and `storage_gdrive` re-exports) |

Every old `Clock` and `FixedClock` path still compiles with the same behaviour. The one difference is `SystemClock` reading the time in one place (`porter-core`) instead of five (syncd, inferd, accountd's two and storage_webdav's `system()`), with identical arithmetic: `i64::try_from(secs).unwrap_or(i64::MAX)`, and 0 before the epoch.

**What stayed, and why.**
- syncd `ManualClock`: a test shares it through an `Arc` and sets it with `&self` (an `AtomicI64`). `porter_core::clock::FixedClock` is `pub UnixSeconds` (tuple syntax `FixedClock(NOW)` is used by about 50 test sites) and sets with `&mut self`, so it cannot be shared and mutated the same way without changing those tests' behaviour. Left in place; it still implements the core trait.
- storage_webdav's `Clock` keeps its struct shape (a value the replicas hold and clone, with `fixed` and `system` constructors). Making it the core trait would change every `Clock::fixed(...)` call; the adapter avoids that.
- The trait stays a plain `fn now(&self)`, dyn-compatible, because accountd holds `Arc<dyn Clock>` (`LoginTiming::clock`). CONVENTIONS 2's "never dyn" rule covers async seams, and this one is synchronous.
- Nothing else changed: no test except its imports (none needed, the paths are kept), no Cargo.toml, no new dependency edge (every crate that re-exports already depends on porter-core).

**Docs.** porter-core's lib doc names `clock` and says `SystemClock` reads the wall clock only when a daemon calls it. ARCHITECTURE section 1's porter-core row lists the clock module; "one home per concept" now says the system clock is `porter-core::clock::SystemClock`, called only by the daemons. CONVENTIONS 4 ("only accountd, syncd and inferd read the system clock") still holds.

**Consumer grep** (`git grep`, origin/master for docket, almanac, cua, mailo, sill, detent and casement; origin/main for anyview; these are the refs as last fetched locally, not re-fetched). Pattern: `porter_service::Clock`, `porter_service::{... Clock ...}`, `porter_fake::{FixedClock}`, `porter_fake::FixedClock`, `storage_webdav::Clock`, `inferd::clock`, `syncd::clock`, `porter_fake::clock`, `porter_service::clock`.
- Zero hits in docket, almanac, cua, sill, detent, casement and anyview.
- mailo: one hit, `crates/mail-app/src/ui/add_account/host.rs:19`, `use porter_service::{AccountService, Clock, ...}`, which still resolves. Its own `SystemClock` (host.rs:148-150, `pub(super) struct SystemClock` implementing `porter_service::Clock`) is the duplicate that can become `porter_core::clock::SystemClock` in a later batch. mailo's two `porter_fake`/`porter_service` files (host.rs, provider.rs) import no fake clock.
- Other repos' own `FixedClock`/`SystemClock` types (docket-fake, almanac-fake, cua-fake, memoryd, cuad, sill-services) are their own traits (`docket_router::Clock`, `almanac`'s `Clock`, `cua`'s `Clock`, sill's `WallClock`), not porter's, and are untouched.

**Verification.** `cargo check --workspace --all-features --tests` passes; `cargo nextest run -p porter-core -p porter-service -p porter-fake -p storage-webdav`: 312 of 312 pass; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` is clean; `cargo fmt --all --check` is clean; `scripts/check-boundary.sh` holds (porter-core and porter-core/src/clock.rs reach no std effects); `cargo metadata --locked` succeeds and Cargo.lock is unchanged.

## Lane layers-peer-client (Whopper F3, F4 and the error half of F5; base `c3a7b4c`, rebased on `5abe1d2`, 2026-10-10)

**1. `porter_client::peer` (feature `dbus`).**
- `PeerAccounts::over(&BusConnection)`: `verdicts(app, need, class, usage) -> Vec<AccountVerdict>`, `resolve_key(grant) -> SecretText` (sealed memfd read once), `report_local(provider, claims, LocalState) -> AccountId`, `news(app) -> AccountNews`.
- `AccountNews::next() -> Option<AccountChange>` (`Accounts`, `Accountd`): subscribes, joins accountd's roster as `app`, joins again when accountd comes back.
- `AccountVerdict` (moved from inferd, same fields), `LocalState {Ok, Offline}`, `chat_need()`, `claim_to_dbus` (moved from inferd).
- `Accounts<DbusTransport>::watch_removals() -> Removals`; `Removals::next() -> Option<RemovedAccount>` (`segment()` is the object path's last segment, a lossy spelling of the id, good as a directory name). The introduction task stops when `Removals` is dropped.
- inferd's `PeerAccountd`, `PeerReports` and `account_news` call it; `AccountdFault` and `ReportFault` stay (what inferd does about a failure) and are built `From<PeerError>`. syncd's `removal::watch(&Connection, ..)` keeps its signature and calls `watch_removals`; it no longer names `ManagerProxy` or `GrantsProxy`. porter-client's `dbus` feature now also takes zbus directly (for the match rule and the owner stream); porter-dbus exports `AccountRemoved` and `AccountRemovedStream`. inferd now depends on porter-client (feature `dbus`): added to check-boundary's EDGES and ARCHITECTURE section 1.
- Behaviour: the same, except `AuthFailed` now counts as a refusal (it was "unreachable" in inferd's two hand-rolled matches).
- Tests: the verdict and key tests moved with the code (`peer::verdict::tests::*`), `peer::tests::a_bus_error_says_whether_to_ask_again`, `removals::tests::*`, inferd's `what_inferd_does_about_each_way_accountd_can_fail` and `report::tests::an_error_name_says_whether_to_ask_again` (now through `PeerError`), and on the bus `porter-client/tests/it/dbus_peer.rs` (a porter daemon reads typed verdicts, others are `Denied`, no accountd is `Unreachable`). `AccountRemoved` is covered by syncd's `wipe_bus`, the news by inferd's `places.rs` EnginesChanged tests.

**2. One classification, one `NoAllowedPlace`.** `PeerError::from(&BusError)` is `porter_dbus::classify` plus `is_invalid_args`; `TransportError`'s `bus_error` is the same `classify`. inferd matches no bus error name any more (the remaining `AccessDenied` strings in inferd are replies it sends). `ClientError::NoAllowedPlace` is gone: `ClientError::Transport(#[from] TransportError)` carries `TransportError::NoAllowedPlace`; `ClientError::no_allowed_place()` reads it. Grep at origin/master (docket, almanac, cua, mailo, sill, detent, casement; anyview at origin/main): nobody matches `ClientError::NoAllowedPlace`, so there is no deprecated path (docket's `Routed::NoAllowedPlace` is its own enum). A match on `ClientError::Transport(_)` now also receives the place refusal; none exists.

**3. Typed errors.** `table_from_toml` returns `CallerTomlError { message }` (same text; `in_file(path)` makes the `CallerFileError`); `load_callers` and `table_from_file` already returned `CallerFileError`. Nobody outside porter calls `table_from_toml` (almanac's memoryd has its own copy). `TransportError::Denied(String)` and `Malformed(String)` are NOT changed in this lane: consumers rely on the strings. cua-eval-cloud `provider.rs:61-64` matches both and formats `Denied(why)`; cuad `tests/it/model.rs:140` and almanac memoryd `tests/it/infer.rs:112` build `Denied(why.clone())`; memoryd `embed.rs:196` passes both `why` as a `String`; detent `add_io.rs:32` matches `Malformed(why) if why.contains("LimitsExceeded")`; sill `app_menu_bus.rs:350` is docket's own type. Proposal for the coordinated batch: `Denied(Denial)` with `Denial { text }` and `Malformed(Malformation)` with `MalformedKind {Bus, Frame, Socket, Reply, Session}` plus the text, both `Deref<Target = str>`, `Display` the same, `From<&str>` and `From<String>`; that keeps `why.contains(..)` and `{why}` working, and breaks only code that builds a variant from a `String` or passes `why` where a `String` is wanted (the three places above), so it needs those consumers' rev bump. A `Busy`/limits variant would replace detent's string test.

**Public changes.** Added: `porter_client::{peer::*, Removals, RemovedAccount}`, `Accounts<DbusTransport>::watch_removals`, `ClientError::no_allowed_place`, `porter_dbus::{AccountRemoved, AccountRemovedStream, CallerTomlError}`, `accountd::CallerTomlError`. Changed: `porter_dbus::table_from_toml`'s error type (no consumer). Removed: `ClientError::NoAllowedPlace`, `From<TransportError> for ClientError` by hand (now derived, same conversion except the place refusal). inferd: `cloud::accountd::AccountVerdict` is a re-export, `PeerAccountd::new` and `PeerReports::new` keep their signatures. Wire and VocabVersion: unchanged.

**Consumers** (built with every porter crate from this worktree, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --offline`): docket, almanac, cua and mailo: all four build clean. sill, detent and casement: grep at origin/master finds no use of anything changed here (detent uses `ClientError::{AlreadyAdded, Refused, Transport}` and `TransportError::Malformed(why)`, both unchanged).

## Lane layers-families (porter-families reads no environment and no system clock; base `5abe1d2`, rebased on `84242b5`, 2026-10-10)

**1. The clock and the paths come from the caller.**
- `MicrosoftEnv::new(http, registry, clock)` and `GoogleEnv::new(http, registry, clock)` take a `porter_core::clock::Clock` (any clock; the family's `Clock` closure is built from it by `clock_of`). `with_clock(closure)` is unchanged, so the rigs' counting clocks still work.
- `MicrosoftEnv::with_client_files(files, clock)` and `GoogleEnv::with_client_files(files, clock)` are the daemon constructors. Removed: `MicrosoftEnv::system()`, `GoogleEnv::system()`, `MicrosoftProvider::new(spec)`, `GoogleProvider::new(spec)` (each read the environment and the clock), `ClientFiles::system()`, and the private `own_clients_path`, `system_now` and `SHIPPED_CLIENTS`. `ClientFiles` is a struct of two public paths, so the caller passes them in directly.
- Behaviour: the same paths and the same times. accountd passes `porter_core::clock::SystemClock` (providers.rs `family_of`) and the `Paths` it already resolved (`Paths::client_files`). accountd's `Paths::resolve` (paths.rs) is the one closure-based environment reader (it is `Xdg`-based like `porter_core::xdg`), so no separate `families_from_env` helper was needed.
- Still read by the families: `/dev/urandom` for PKCE (`system_random`, the `Random` default). It is randomness, not the environment or the clock; the brief did not list it. Left as is.
- The removed `own_clients_path` test covered the XDG-then-HOME rule; accountd's `paths.rs` tests cover the same rule through `Paths::resolve` (`xdg_variables_win_and_relative_ones_are_ignored`, `the_families_read_the_clients_file_settings_writes`).
- Tests: the constructors gained a clock argument, so the call sites changed beyond their imports: `tests/it/google/rig.rs` and `tests/it/microsoft/rig.rs` pass a `FixedClock` that their own `with_clock` replaces; `tests/it/microsoft/signin.rs` and accountd's `tests/it/google.rs` pass `SystemClock` (what they used before); the two in-module tests of `google/env.rs` pass a `FixedClock`. No assertion changed.
- Deprecated wrappers: none. Grep at origin/master (docket, almanac, cua, mailo, detent, sill, casement; anyview at origin/main) for `porter_families`, `MicrosoftEnv`, `GoogleEnv`, `MicrosoftProvider::new`, `GoogleProvider::new`, `ClientFiles::system` and `own_clients_path` finds no hit. mailo mentions porter-families only in a comment (`mail-app/src/ui/add_account/provider.rs:13`) and does not depend on it.

**2. check-boundary checks porter-families per family feature.** Default features are empty, so the old rule only proved the empty set. Each feature listed under `[features]` in `crates/porter-families/Cargo.toml` now has a row in `FAMILY_FORBIDS` (what it may not reach) and `FAMILY_EDGES` (its exact direct porter-* dependencies). A feature with no row fails the check. Measured with `cargo tree -p porter-families --no-default-features --features <f> -e normal,build --offline`:

| feature | reaches | direct porter-* edges |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `nextcloud` | none | porter-core, porter-dav, porter-discover, porter-http, porter-provider |
| `generic` | tokio (through porter-proxy's `tls`, which implies `io`) | the above, plus porter-proxy |
| `microsoft` | tokio, hyper (porter-oauth `io`, porter-http `hyper`) | porter-core, porter-discover, porter-http, porter-oauth, porter-provider |
| `google` | tokio, hyper | porter-core, porter-http, porter-oauth, porter-provider |
| `api_key` | tokio, hyper (porter-http `hyper`) | porter-core, porter-http, porter-provider |
| `agent_login` | none | porter-core, porter-http, porter-provider |
| `openrouter` | none (porter-oauth without `io`) | porter-core, porter-http, porter-oauth, porter-provider |
| `tailnet` | tokio, hyper (porter-tailscale `io`) | porter-core, porter-http, porter-provider, porter-tailscale |

Every other forbidden effect (`zbus`, `zvariant`, `reqwest`, `ureq`, `oo7`, `keyring`, `secret-service`, `interprocess`, `latchkey`, `ds-settings`) is forbidden for every feature. The default-feature rule is unchanged. No other rule changed. `check-boundary.sh` passes, with no LEAK, ERROR or EDGE line.

**3. ARCHITECTURE.md.** The sentence that said `porter-families` reaches tokio "through none" now lists what each family feature reaches (the table above, in words), and the boundary table's `porter-families` row names the features that switch on `porter-oauth`'s `io`, `porter-http`'s `hyper`, `porter-proxy`'s `tls` and `porter-tailscale`'s `io`. The `dispatch` row says the clock is the caller's.

**Verification.** `cargo check --workspace --all-features --tests` passes with no warnings; `cargo nextest run -p porter-families --all-features`: 164 of 164 pass; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` is clean; `cargo fmt --all --check` is clean after `cargo fmt --all` (line breaks in the lines this lane touched); `scripts/check-boundary.sh` holds (exit 0); `cargo metadata --locked` succeeds.

**Consumers.** mailo at origin/master (detached worktree; its `.cargo/config.toml` patches its ten porter git crates to this worktree). `cargo check --workspace --offline -v` passes. Ten porter crates compiled from this worktree (`porter-core`, `porter-provider`, `porter-secrets`, `porter-discover`, `porter-oauth`, `porter-http`, `porter-fs`, `porter-service`, `porter-client`, `porter-dbus`, `porter-proxy`). `porter-families` did not compile there: cargo reports its patch "was not used in the crate graph", because mailo does not depend on it. mailo's `map.rs` `fault_of` needed three `SignInFault` arms (`NotRunning`, `SignedOut`, `NotAllowed`) that the pinned quire rev's `ShellFault` lacks; they were added to the check worktree only, as stand-ins (`Unreachable`, `Refused`, `Forbidden`). Not committed to mailo.

## Lane layers-daemon-config (Whopper F6 and F7; base `84242b5`, rebased on `831bb01`, 2026-10-10)

**Each daemon's lib has a `daemon` module** (re-exported at the crate root): `Config`, `Daemon::build`, `Daemon::run`, `StartError`.
- accountd: `Config { paths, keys: Selection, proc_root: Option<PathBuf>, runtime_dir: Option<PathBuf>, bus: BusTarget }`. `Config::from_env(provider_dirs)` reads HOME and the XDG variables, `XDG_DATA_DIRS`, `ACCOUNTD_TAILSCALE_SOCKET` (all through `Paths::resolve`), `ACCOUNTD_KEYS`, `ACCOUNTD_PROC_ROOT` and `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`. `Daemon::build(Config)` loads everything and serves (when it returns the name is owned); `run(self, shutdown: impl Future)` closes the connection once shutdown completes. `StartError::exit_code()` keeps the refused-registry status. `add_account(&Config, provider, allow, classes)` is `accountd add`'s logic (the printing and exit code stay in the bin).
- syncd: `Config { paths, proc_root, pim: PimConfig, storage: StorageConfig (rescan, photos), bus }`. `from_env()` reads HOME and the XDG variables, `SYNCD_PROC_ROOT`, `SYNCD_RESCAN_S` (test builds only, as before) and `SYNCD_PHOTOS`. `Daemon::build(Config, watch::Receiver<Network>)` takes the network as a second argument (the brief's `build(Config)` plus the receiver F7 asks for); the stub with the dropped-sender-kept channel is in main.rs only. `run` ends both supervisors and closes the connection.
- inferd: `Config { dirs, config_file, tailscale_socket: Option<PathBuf>, proc_root: ProcRoot, bus }`. `from_env(config_file)` reads `Dirs::from_env` (HOME, XDG, `HF_HOME`), `INFERD_TAILSCALE_SOCKET` and `INFERD_PROC_ROOT`. `run(self, stop: impl Shutdown) -> Result<(), StopError>`; `shutdown::Shutdown` is new (implemented by `Signals` and by `mpsc::UnboundedReceiver<()>`), and `on_signal` is generic over it. main.rs still starts `Signals::listen()` before anything else.
- porter-dbus gets `BusTarget { Session, Address(String) }` with `connect()`, so a `Config` names its bus and the daemon reads no `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` itself.
- Every `Config` is `#[non_exhaustive]` with `new(..)` and `with_*` builders.

**Env reads.** `grep "std::env\|env::var" crates/{accountd,syncd,inferd}/src` now finds them only in `Config::from_env`, `Dirs::from_env` and `Chosen::from_env` (kept as a public path), plus `temp_dir()` in test code. `check-boundary.sh` fails on any `env::var`/`var_os`/`vars`/`args`/`current_dir` outside a fn named `from_env` in those three libs (main.rs and test files excluded).

**Tests** (private bus, no env var read, in the jail): `accountd::daemon::a_daemon_built_from_a_config_serves_its_name_and_stops_when_told`, and the same name in `syncd` and `inferd` (tests/it/daemon.rs). Each builds from a `Config` over scratch paths, checks the name is owned and answers a Ping, runs, stops, and waits (porter_fake::Deadline) for the name to go. The syncd dist test that grepped main.rs for `rescan(BUILD, rescan_var` now greps daemon.rs.

**Behaviour differences.** The order of start-up steps is unchanged. accountd opens its secret store at the top of `build` (it did in main, right after the paths); a `test-keys` file that cannot be opened is now reported by `build`, not by `from_env`. inferd closes its connection after a clean stop (the process used to exit). Consumers: none of docket, almanac, cua, mailo, sill, detent, casement (origin/master) or anyview (origin/main) depends on accountd, syncd or inferd (grep of Cargo.toml and `use` lines).

## Lane layers-client-feature (Whopper F2: porter-client's `in-process` feature; base `326564f`, 2026-10-10)

**The feature.** `porter-client` feature `in-process` gates `InProcess` (`transport/in_process.rs`) and its dependencies `porter-service`, `porter-secrets` and `porter-provider`, which are now optional and listed again as dev-dependencies so the tests keep them. `default = ["infer", "in-process"]`, so nothing changes for a default consumer. `SessionHost`, `InProcessSession` and `NoBroker` moved to `transport/broker.rs`, which needs no service, so `engines` and `infer` without `in-process` still build. `porter-rig` names `in-process` explicitly (it does not use `InProcess` itself). The tests that use `InProcess` (`end_to_end`, `in_process_session`, `prepare`) carry the feature in their file-level `cfg`. `check-boundary.sh` has a new block: `cargo tree -p porter-client --no-default-features --features dbus` reaches none of porter-service, porter-secrets or porter-provider (checked non-vacuous: with `in-process` added, porter-service appears).

**Package counts** (`cargo tree -p porter-client -e normal,build`, unique packages):
- default (`infer`, `in-process`): 32; `--no-default-features --features infer` (no in-process): 22;
- `--features dbus,infer`: 71 without `in-process`, 78 with it (+7);
- `--features dbus` alone: 66.

- Does any consumer use `porter_client::InProcess`? No. docket, almanac and cua have their own `InProcess` types (docket-client, almanac-client); mailo's `porter-client` is `default-features = false`, and its only `InProcess` mentions are in comments. So the follow-up to turn `in-process` off by default needs no consumer change; mailo already builds without it.
- docket: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --offline` passes; every porter crate compiled from the worktree (the first run's log). The docket-accept build script runs `cargo install --git`, which needs the network, so the check set `ACCEPT_PORTER_DIR` and `ACCEPT_ALMANAC_DIR` to the local checkouts.
- almanac: passes; porter-client, -core, -dbus, -fs, -infer, -provider, -secrets, -service and prov compiled from the worktree.
- cua: passes; the same nine crates compiled from the worktree.
- mailo: FAILS, and not because of this lane. `porter-core`'s `SignInFault` gained `NotRunning`, `SignedOut` and `NotAllowed` in `fd823ec` (tailnet-account), and mailo's `crates/mail-app/src/ui/add_account/map.rs:842` (`fault_of`) matches it exhaustively. porter-core is byte-identical between `326564f` and this lane's tip, so the same error occurs on the base. Mailo needs three arms (or `_`) before it can take porter master. Not fixed here: it is mailo's repo.

**Open.**

It predates this lane (the methods serve only the socket carrier), and the gate builds with `--all-features`, so it does not fail there. Owner: the next lane that touches `framed.rs`.
