## Lane test-cut-porter (cuts, merges, binaries and speed-ups; base `326564f`, 2026-10-10)

Plan `~/rs-wt/test-cut/porter.md`, rule `~/rs-wt/test-cut/RUBRIC.md`. Every row was re-grepped at the base first.

| | before (`326564f`) | after |
|---|---|---|
| tests (nextest, jailed gate) | 2608 (+1 skipped) | 2609 (+1 skipped) |
| test binaries | 66 (32 integration) | 60 (26 integration) |
| gate test run, wall | 47.1 s | 5.5 s (11.1 s and 13.8 s at the two steps before; the machine is shared) |
| slowest test | photos 1000, 42.6 s | 3.4 s (inferd engine_start) |

The test count is +1 net because the cuts (-8) and merges (-17) are matched by the 26 guard tests and the guard's own unit test (+26).

**Cuts** (commit 1): eight tests, six assertions. Each cut's covering test was opened and named in the commit message. Plan rows not applied: `the_system_random_gives_fresh_bytes` stays (K4: the only test that the PKCE/state random is not a constant); the google and microsoft signin tables stay apart (each has its own copy of `refusal` and `loopback_fault`, so there is no shared mapper to table); google_pim_bus and graph_calendar_bus "local edits are never sent" stay (their never-send assertion is per provider fake; only the shared mirror half is the same as pim_bus's). The accountd/syncd `paths.rs` shared names are two separate codes, a layers note only.

**Merges** (commit 2): seventeen tests into six tables or a scenario; every case is a named row or a numbered step. porter-proxy now has `login.rs` with one table per login behaviour and one row per protocol (imap, pop3, smtp); sieve stays apart (its fake records logins in another shape). The new smtp rows (untrusted certificate, nothing listening) are cases the old tests did not have.

**Binaries** (commit 3): 32 to 26. inferd's four loose files, porter-client `connect` and porter-rig `secrets` are modules of their `tests/it`; the fake-child names are module paths. `socket_accounts` stays its own binary and says why. `porter_fake::guard::every_module_is_declared` (with its own unit test) is called by a one-line test in every `tests/it/main.rs`; twelve crates gained `porter-fake` as a dev-dependency for it (a dev-dependency cycle is fine for cargo; the boundary script only reads normal and build edges). CONVENTIONS item 5 says so.

**Speed-ups.**
- Item 1, the thousand photos: 42.6 s to 0.8 s in the gate (1.7 s alone), still 1000. The cause was not the disk, the build profile or the listing: B's download made 44 ms a request, which is Nagle's algorithm against the peer's delayed acknowledgement on loopback (the fake servers answered in two writes and the rig dialled without `TCP_NODELAY`). Fixed in porter-fake-servers `net.rs` (every accepted and dialled connection) and the photos rig's dial. (a) dependencies at opt-level 2 and the /dev/shm half of (b) changed nothing and are not applied; `synchronous=OFF` through `Journal::open_unflushed` (cfg(test)) took 1.5 s more off and is applied. (d) 520 photos was not needed and not done.
- Item 2, polls in tenths of a second: not a millisecond `Settings` (the scheduler dates in `UnixSeconds`) but `Settings::time_scale` (scheduler seconds per real second, 1 in the daemon), `clock::Wall` (the engine clock that follows it; scale 1 is exactly `SystemClock`) and `driver::step` dividing by it. The three supervisors and the photos run build their engine clock from `settings.time_scale`. `Settings::quick()` replaces thirteen copies of the same literal and sets 10.
- Item 5, the fixed "nothing happens" sleeps: they became `common::poll_time(ms)`, the same number of scheduler seconds. I did not add a cycle counter: the status is a wire dictionary, and a counter in it would be a vocabulary change. The cost: on a loaded machine these waits cover fewer polls, so the proof is thinner, never flaky. **Left for the owner**: a cycle counter in the hub (not in the wire status) would let these wait for N cycles instead.
- Item 3, the torn-read payload (pim_bus, K1): 4000 padding lines a file to 1000 (58 KB, the other two providers use 30 KB); three rounds of twenty and `reads > 200` unchanged. Not batched.
- Item 9, the hosts 1.5 s health test: `start_paused`; the 1.5 s and the probe's own timeout stay in play. 1.5 s to 0.004 s.
- Item 10: oo7 has no setting for fewer PBKDF2 rounds (it refuses fewer than its minimum), so the crypto crates (pbkdf2, hmac, sha2, digest, aes, cbc, cipher and their blocks) build at opt-level 3 in dev instead: `oo7_file_backend_keeps_the_contract` 1.46 s to 0.28 s.
- **Not done, with why.** Item 6 (the 400 ms after `Pause`): the pause shows in Status at once, so Status cannot tell that an in-flight cycle has ended; the sleep is a settle for a cycle, and a shorter one would add a race that appears only under load. Item 7 (engine_start 3.1 s to 0.45 s): the same test asserts that three requests inside the pause start nothing, which a 400 ms pause would turn into a race under load. Item 8 (HELD 2 s to 500 ms): HELD is the daemon's artificial start-up delay, and the test must make its call before it ends: proxy and a 300 ms sleep take about 0.6 s on a quiet machine, so 500 ms would let the held runtime go first and the K1 proof pass without proving anything. All three tests stay as they are; the three `a_call_made_as_soon_as_the_name_is_owned_is_answered` at 2.87 s and engine_start at 3.4 s now set the wall.

**Found on the way.** `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `--all-features`) fails at the base on porter-client (`write_body` and `read_body_with_fds` never used without the dbus feature); the gate uses `--all-features` and is clean. Another lane (layers-client-feature) is on it.

## Lane fix-loadflake-3 (the last fixed caps; base `5bdda62`, 2026-10-10)

**The failure.** `syncd::it pim_bus::a_reader_never_sees_a_file_that_is_not_a_complete_item` (K1, kept with every assert) waited at most 90 s per round of twenty large items. On the load-75 gate its sibling bus tests took 180 to 226 s, so the cap failed a daemon that was still mirroring. The round wait is now idle time: `porter_fake::Deadline::idle(GENEROUS)` with `progress()`, and the wait counts mirrored items and fails only when that count stops growing for 120 s. `Deadline::after` is unchanged for fixed waits. The deadline's own unit test no longer times a 30 ms sleep against a 30 ms limit (that could fail on a loaded machine).

**Audit of fixed caps** (`from_secs`, `from_millis`, `timeout(`, `within(`, `eventually`, `Timeout` across `crates/*/tests` and `src`, about 530 lines):
- Converted to `porter_fake::GENEROUS` (positive waits, the event should come): accountd `start` (the first call), `relay` (`read_until`), `spaces` (`next_change`, was 2 s), `google` (a read, was 10 s), `start` and inferd `voice_chat` (20 s), `speech` (20 s, 10 s), `probed` (10 s), `places` (5 s signal wait), `attached` and `shutdown` (`exit_within`, 5 to 20 s, the window is gone from the call; the `inferd takes its name` loop of 20 s is a `Deadline`), syncd `start` (60 s), `sync_bus` `next` (60 s), `photos_picker_bus` (60 s), `porter-proxy` common `WAIT` (10 s), porter-client `process_credential` (5 s), `dbus_spaces` (2 s), porter-service `add_flow` (5 s), porter-rig client read (10 s).
- Left, and why: waits that prove something does NOT happen, each with a doc that says so (accountd `told`, `hears_nothing`, `heard(.., 300 ms)`, `quiet_for`; inferd `voice_chat` 400 ms "no more events"; accountd `process_credential` 300 ms). Durations that ARE the behaviour under test: accountd `start` HELD (2 s), syncd `start` HELD, inferd `engine_start` FAST (25 s, "well under 30 s and 180 s"), `bus_sheets` slow sheet (2 s), accountd `agent_launcher` BOUND (30 s, the launcher's own answer bound), inferd `shutdown` BOUND (15 s, stop bound: systemd's `TimeoutStopSec` is 20 s). Backoff and poll configuration of the product (porter-families `first`/`step`/`limit`, `LOOK_EVERY`, `poll_slice`, the Google picker's own 5 s and 600 s). Drains ending at a quiet window: `porter-tailnet` and accountd `drain`/`scenario_fixes` `all_changes` (1 s quiet, see the risk below). Unbounded `while !name_has_owner` loops with no literal cap: left, nextest's 600 s terminate-after is their backstop.
- **Left with a risk (not changed).** accountd `scenario_fixes::all_changes` ends the list at the first 1 s without a `Changed`; a change that comes late under load is missed and the exact-list assert fails. The fix is an expected count (wait GENEROUS for each expected change, then a quiet window for extras); three callers need their counts. Not done in this lane.
- **Left with a timing claim (not changed).** inferd `shutdown` asserts `elapsed() < 5 s` and `< 4 s` after a stop with a 5 s bound; porter-proxy `tls.rs` asserts a 150 ms handshake timeout fires within 5 s. Both can fail on a starved machine. They prove the stop or timeout does not wait for its full bound; widening them to the product bound would weaken what they prove.

**Product check (item 3).**
- syncd: no wall-clock limit on a person's path would fail under load. The bounds that exist are `STOP_WITHIN` (300 s), `RELAY_IDLE` (60 s), the supervisors' rescans (600 s), the Google picker's own poll and timeout (5 s and 600 s, from the service). Left as they are.
- **Changed: porter-client `START_WAIT`, 5 s to 30 s.** A client waits this long for an agent it just spawned (`latchkey::connect_or_start`). A loaded machine can take several seconds to exec and load it; the client then reported the agent unreachable. The wait ends at the answer, so it costs only a start that really failed. Row: `socket_accounts::a_spawning_client_starts_the_agent_and_then_talks_to_it` now passes `START_WAIT` as its spawn wait (it used 120 s), so the consumer default runs through a real spawn. That test does not delay the child, so it does not prove the 30 s under starvation; that would need a child that waits before listening.
- **Changed: porter-families generic sign-in relay, `WITHIN`, 15 s to 60 s.** It bounds the dial, the TLS handshake and the login together; on a loaded machine or a slow network a legitimate sign-in can exceed 15 s and be reported as unreachable. No existing test reaches the limit (the refused and unreachable cases fail at once), so there is no row for it. A test with a server that accepts and then goes quiet would be the row to add; not written in this lane.
- **Left: porter-tailscale client's 10 s timeout** (a local Tailscale query, `client.rs:79`). No evidence it fails under load; it is a candidate for the same treatment.
- **Left: Windows `BUSY_FOR` 2 s** (porter-client `socket/windows.rs`): a pipe-busy retry window. It is `cfg(windows)`, so it was not compiled or run here; it needs the same look on a Windows host.
- **Left: `lend.rs:379` 10 s** (refuse a connection that sends nothing): a product bound that does not wait on the person; a slow client that sends its first bytes after 10 s is refused, which is by design.

**Crowded run (`--profile crowded`, 128 threads), jailed, on the tip `41f6479`.**
- `package(syncd)`: 263 run, 263 passed, 9.9 s. Load average (1 min) at the start 32.3, at the end 32.2.
- Whole workspace: 2610 run, 2610 passed, 1 skipped, 12.9 s. Load average at the start 30.2, at the end 33.3.
- Neither run reproduced a failure. The machine was at about 30 during these runs, well below the 75 to 150 of the failing gates, so this run is not evidence of the fix at that load. I did not repeat the reproduction under a CPU-limited `systemd-run` scope (the Part 1 method) in this lane.

**Gate.** `~/rs-wt/gate-lane-jailed.sh ~/rs-wt/fix-loadflake-3/porter` on `41f6479`: LANE GREEN. Load average (1 min) at the start 149.7 (the previous lanes' builds), at the end 34.2.

**Commits (author and committer Po Hsuan Lai):**
- `96ff2a5` syncd pim_bus: the round wait is idle time, not a fixed 90 s cap (`Deadline::idle`, `progress()`, unit test).
- `6ea06ad` tests: fixed caps on waits for something to happen become `porter_fake::GENEROUS`.
- `41f6479` product: `START_WAIT` 30 s, the sign-in `WITHIN` 60 s.

**Sheet race (after the rebase onto `b12886e`, gate at load 41 to 114).** `porter-client::it accountd_requests::a_call_with_no_token_gets_a_path_under_its_callers_namespace` asked for a second sheet of one kind before the first had closed, and got `LimitsExceeded`. That is the product's rule (one sheet of a kind per app), so the test was wrong: it now subscribes to the sheet, waits for the first sheet's Response (dismissed, code 1), and only then asks again. Both namespace asserts and the `assert_ne` are kept. Sweep of every sheet-opening call in `tests/it`: the other sequential asks wait for their Response (`common::choose`, `world.choose` in accountd google, `Sheet::response` in the rest); the deliberate second asks (accountd_requests "one sheet of a kind at a time", bus_sheets "a second sheet of a kind while one is open is refused") are refusals under test and stay.

## Lane client-guests (Whopper's ask for sill batch 10 and detent: typed guest and computer calls; base `5f1ded4`, 2026-10-10)

**Where the types live, and why.** The plain rows moved to a new module `porter_core::lending`: `GuestAnswer`, `RowState`, `GuestRow` and `Approval` (moved from porter-tailnet, each keeping its old path there as a `pub use`) and the new `GuestAsk`, `CandidateModel` and `ComputerCandidate`. porter-tailnet was not reused as it is: without `io` it has no tokio, hyper or sockets, but it reaches `porter-fs`, `porter-tailscale`, `toml`, `toml_datetime`, `toml_parser`, `toml_writer`, `winnow`, `serde_spanned` and itself, nine packages that a bus client has no use for (the file the answers are kept in is the lending daemon's). **Package count porter-client gains: 0** (`cargo tree -p porter-client -e normal`, unique lines: `--no-default-features --features dbus` 83 before and after; `dbus,infer` 89; no features 20). `NodeId` was already in porter-core and is reused. The new core types are not on the wire (no serde), so `VocabVersion` is unchanged (12).

**API** (all in `porter_client`; the module `porter_client::lending` also holds `Approval`, `RowState`, `GuestAsk`, `CandidateModel`):
- `Accounts<T: Transport>`:
  - `guests() -> Result<Vec<GuestRow>, ClientError>`; `GuestRow { node: NodeId, name: String, state: RowState (Approved, Denied, Asking), since: UnixSeconds }`;
  - `answer_guest(&NodeId, GuestAnswer) -> Result<(), ClientError>`; `GuestAnswer { Allow, Deny }`, on the wire the `answer` slug `allow` / `deny` (`GuestAnswer::slug`, `from_slug`);
  - `forget_guest(&NodeId)`;
  - `candidates() -> Result<Vec<ComputerCandidate>, ClientError>`; `ComputerCandidate { node, name, models: Vec<CandidateModel { id, name }>, approval: Approval (Needed, Given) }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, with `new`);
  - feature `infer` (it returns a `PlaceId`): `add_tailnet_computer(&NodeId) -> PlaceId`, `add_computer(&NewComputer) -> PlaceId` and `remove_computer(&ComputerName)`. `NewComputer { name: String, models: Vec<NewComputerModel { model: ModelId, reach: ComputerReach (Socket(PathBuf) | Port(u16)), key: Option<SecretText> }> }`, all `#[non_exhaustive]` with `new` and `with_key`. `PlaceId` and `ComputerName` are re-exported from porter-infer.
- `Accounts<DbusTransport>::watch_guests()` and `Accounts<AnyTransport>::watch_guests()` (the second is `Unsupported` off the bus) give `GuestChanges`, a `BusStream` with `next().await -> Option<GuestChange>`; `GuestChange` is `#[non_exhaustive]`: `Asks { node: NodeId, ask: GuestAsk { name, since } }` and `Changed`. Both signals are broadcast, so nothing is called first. A `GuestAsks` whose node or details are not plain is `Changed` (the listener reads the list again and sees the question). Subscribe before reading `guests()`.
- Errors: `TransportError::Unsupported` (new; the default of the new `Transport` methods, so the socket link and `InProcess` answer it, and an implementor outside the crate still compiles) and `TransportError::Computer { reason: ComputerReason, words: String }` (new; inferd's `org.quire.Inference1.Error.Computer.<Name>` with its plain sentence in `words`; `ComputerReason` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, one variant per name inferd sends, and `Other(String)` for a name this client does not know). A row that does not say what the interface promises is `TransportError::Malformed`. All additive: `TransportError` and `ClientError` were `#[non_exhaustive]` already. porter-dbus gets `computer_refusal_of(&zbus::Error)` and exports `GuestAsks`, `GuestAsksStream`, `GuestsChanged`, `GuestsChangedStream`.
- InProcess: all of these are `Unsupported` (inferd's lib exposes no cheap path to its computers and guests, and an app that hosts the core itself has no Tailscale account in it).

**For sill and detent.** Example: `crates/porter-client/examples/guests.rs` (`cargo run -p porter-client --example guests --features dbus -- allow`): subscribes, lists the guests, answers the first one that is asking, then prints the changes. detent builds porter-client with `default-features = false, features = ["dbus"]`: `guests`, `answer_guest`, `forget_guest`, `candidates` and `watch_guests` need nothing more; the three computer-adding calls need `features = ["dbus", "infer"]`, which adds no package to detent (it already depends on porter-infer). sill's batch-9 `InferenceProxy` use can move to `Accounts::answer_guest`; the refusal sill treated as quiet (`NotAsking`) is `Err(ClientError::Transport(TransportError::Computer { reason: ComputerReason::NotAsking, .. }))`.

**Tests** (`crates/porter-client/tests/it/dbus_guests.rs`, in the existing binary, against a scripted `org.quire.Inference1` on a private bus): `guests_decode_into_typed_rows`, `a_guest_row_that_says_less_than_promised_is_malformed_not_a_panic` (bad state slug, bad node id, missing keys), `the_answer_crosses_as_its_slug_and_forgetting_names_the_node` (Allow is `allow`, Deny is `deny`), `a_refusal_comes_back_as_a_typed_reason_with_the_sentence` (a known name and an unknown one), `candidates_decode_with_their_models_and_approval`, `a_tailnet_computer_is_added_by_node_and_answers_its_place`, `a_computer_added_by_hand_sends_how_to_reach_each_model_and_is_removed_by_name` (port, socket and key arguments), `the_stream_hears_of_a_computer_asking_and_of_the_answers_changing` (Asks, Changed, a malformed ask read as Changed), `a_caller_the_daemon_refuses_is_denied_and_no_daemon_is_unreachable`; and `the_guest_and_computer_calls_are_unsupported_in_process` in `in_process_session.rs`. They assert values and run only in the jailed gate.

**Consumers** (check worktrees at origin/master in `~/rs-wt/client-guests`; docket 5964800, almanac c6effb7, cua 6150644, mailo e958659 by git pin with a `[patch."https://github.com/PoHsuanLai/porter"]` over every porter crate in the lock, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --offline`; sill 07bdf95d `--locked --features sill/debug`, detent 1d875e5; each log shows "Checking <crate> v0.1.0 (.../client-guests/porter/...)" for every one):
- docket: passes; porter-client, -core, -dbus, -fake, -infer, -provider, -secrets, -service and prov from the worktree (ACCEPT_PORTER_DIR and ACCEPT_ALMANAC_DIR set, as in the earlier lane, so the accept build script needs no network).
- almanac: passes; client, core, dbus, infer, provider, secrets, service, prov from the worktree.
- cua: passes; the same eight.
- mailo: passes (the `SignInFault` arms landed on its master); client, core, dbus, discover, http, oauth, provider, proxy, secrets, service from the worktree.
- sill: passes with `--locked`; core, dbus, infer, prov from the worktree. Run in `~/rs-wt/client-guests/b10/`, where `porter` is a symlink to this worktree and docket, almanac, cua, quire, anyview, blitz-kit, keycap, shell-host and palmrest are the batch-9 checkouts of `~/rs-wt/wh-batch9` (the shared `~/quire` is behind sill's pin, and the newer docket and almanac at origin/master make sill's lock drift: both unrelated to porter).
- detent: passes with `--offline`, not `--locked`: its `Cargo.lock` still lists `porter-provider`, `porter-secrets` and `porter-service` under porter-client, and loses them (the `in-process` feature, lane layers-client-feature, not this one); detent's owner re-locks. client, core, dbus, infer, provider, prov from the worktree.

**Open.** None for this lane. `GuestChanges` is a D-Bus stream; a socket carrier has no guests, so its `watch_guests` stays `Unsupported` until inferd has a socket.
## Lane feature-builds (the consumers' feature sets; base `df1b8c5`, 2026-10-10)

**Why.** Porter 5bdda62 broke docket's batch-9 gate. docket builds inferd on its own (`cargo install --git porter`, `-D warnings`) with porter-client on `dbus` and `infer` and no `socket`. Two `framed.rs` methods were then unused. The workspace gate builds `--workspace --all-features`, which unifies every feature, so it never saw that build. b12886e fixed the code. This lane closes the gap.

**What changed.**
- `scripts/check-features.sh` (`548d7d4`, `343c24f`): clippy with `-D warnings` for each set below, one line per set (`ok <set>` or `FAIL <set>`), exit status is the verdict. Logs go to `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR/check-features/`. Builds `--lib --bins`, the targets a consumer compiles. Test targets are left out on purpose; see the follow-up below.
- CI: a `features` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the script. It is not in `check-boundary.sh`, so each gate does not pay for it (the hook added in `e843636` was undone in the next commit).
- The coordinator runs the script before each push.

**The 19 sets and their consumers** (from each consumer's manifest at origin/master):
- `inferd`, `accountd`, `syncd` (default features, each with `-p` alone). inferd is docket's sibling binary; accountd and syncd are the desktop's.
- porter-client, no features (mailo's pure client). `dbus` (detent's Settings). `dbus,infer` with no default features (inferd's build as docket makes it). `dbus` with the defaults (inferd, syncd, docket, almanac, cua). `socket` and `socket,infer` (the socket carrier for other desktops and macOS; no consumer found in origin/master manifests). Defaults (docket, almanac, cua). `engines` (no consumer found; kept so it cannot break unseen).
- porter-secrets: `testing` (mailo mail-core, mail-app); `oo7` (mail-runtime, Linux); `keyring` (mail-runtime, macOS and Windows).
- porter-discover and porter-http, no features (mailo mail-core, mail-runtime).
- porter-oauth, no features (mailo); `io` (mail-runtime).
- porter-proxy, `io` (mail-runtime, its link tests).

**Proof that it catches the docket break.** On a scratch branch (`feature-builds-proof`, not committed), I reverted b12886e's three `cfg(feature = "socket")` lines with Edit and ran the script. Output in `~/rs-wt/feature-builds/check-features-proof-revert.out`:
- `FAIL porter-client (dbus,infer: inferd's build as docket makes it)`: "methods `write_body` and `read_body_with_fds` are never used", with `-D warnings`.
- `FAIL porter-client (dbus with defaults ...)`, `FAIL inferd (default)` and `FAIL syncd (default)` with the same error.
- The socket sets and the no-features set stayed `ok`, as expected. The file was then restored (`git checkout`), and the scratch branch was left in place.

**Master.** The script passes on master: `exit=0`, all 19 `ok` (`~/rs-wt/feature-builds/check-features-master2.out`).

**Wall time.** About 6 minutes of clippy at load 70 to 110 (`6m03s`; the earlier all-targets run took 4m46s at load about 74). That is why it is not in the boundary check.

**Deviation from the brief.** The brief said `--all-targets`. With it, master failed on `accountd` (default features): dead code in `accountd`'s `tests/it/relay.rs` (`Tap`, `start`, `drain`, `contains`) under `-D warnings`. Those helpers are dead in a default build, and no consumer compiles that test target. The script builds `--lib --bins`.

**Follow-up (not fixed here).** The `accountd` `tests/it/relay.rs` helpers (`Tap`, `start`, `drain`, `contains`) are dead under default features. A fix would `cfg` them to the features the tests need, or move them into a module those tests use. The lint job's `--all-targets --all-features` clippy does not catch this, because all features are on there.

## Lane layers-http-stream (the HTTP/1.1 stream client and framing, into porter-http; base `80ad4a1`, 2026-10-10)

**What moved.** From storage-webdav to `porter_http::stream` (feature `stream`): `StreamHttp`, `Dial`, `StreamLimits` (`stream/client.rs`, from `stream_http.rs`) and the HTTP/1.1 framing (`stream/wire.rs`, from `wire.rs`, with its tests), with the module doc in `stream.rs`. Code is byte for byte the same apart from its imports. `DELETED` stays in storage-webdav: it is the version a removed WebDAV item has (`"deleted"`), replica vocabulary, so it is not HTTP-generic. The `Clock` stays too.

**Why the feature is `stream` and not `hyper`.** The framing reads porter-core's `ByteStream`, which has no runtime, so the module needs no dependency at all: `stream = []`. Putting it behind `hyper` would have pulled tokio into the stream client for no reason. check-boundary has a rule for it (porter-http with `--no-default-features --features stream` reaches none of the effect crates), and ARCHITECTURE section 1 says so.

**Old paths and re-exports.** storage-webdav re-exports `Dial`, `StreamHttp` and `StreamLimits` from `porter_http::stream`, each with a `#[doc]` line, for one batch. storage-graph and storage-gdrive re-export the same three from `porter_http::stream` (so `storage_graph::StreamHttp` still resolves) and keep `Clock` and `DELETED` from storage-webdav. Only those three crates enable `porter-http/stream`.

**syncd.** Unchanged, since every import goes through an old path. `datasets/pim/source/google/calendar.rs` imports only `Clock` and `DELETED`, which do not move, so it has no edit. The import rewrite (ten files: `datasets/pim/relay.rs`, `webdav.rs`, `graph.rs`, `gdrive.rs`, `datasets/photos/google/wiring.rs`, `datasets/pim/source/google/mod.rs`, `datasets/pim/source/graph/mod.rs`, `datasets/pim/source/dav.rs`, `datasets/photos/tests/rig.rs`, `tests/it/stalled.rs`) is for a later batch, once the old paths go.

**The other HTTP/1.1 framers** (named and read, none changed):
- inferd `agent/http.rs`: the server side (reads one request, with `Content-Length` or chunks and `Expect: 100-continue`, over tokio's `AsyncRead`/`AsyncWrite`; writes JSON or a chunked event stream). It duplicates `find`, `fill`, `parse_head` and `read_chunked` from `wire.rs`. A request-side framer in porter-http would let it share them. Later.
- porter-proxy `http1.rs`, `http1/head.rs`, `http1/chunked.rs`: the relay. It rewrites request heads and passes bodies through unchanged, so it needs where a chunked body ends (`ChunkParser`), not a response reader. Keep it; share the chunk-end rule only if a second caller appears.
- porter-fake-servers `http/mod.rs` and `http/client.rs`: the fakes' server, and a test client whose `exchange` writes its own head and reads a response. The client is the one that could become a `StreamHttp` over a tokio stream adapter. Test-only, later.
- porter-rig `client.rs`: `talk` (writes one request to a descriptor and reads until `complete`). A client reader over a descriptor, so a `Dial` over `OwnedFd` could serve it. Later, and it needs a `ByteStream` for a descriptor first.
- inferd `replay/engine.rs` and `replay/render.rs`: the replay server on a Unix socket (reads one request with `Content-Length`, writes responses). Server side, test tooling; no fit.
- porter-oauth `loopback_io.rs`: reads one loopback `GET` head and answers a short page with `Connection: close`. Not a body framer; no fit.
- Writers of fixed canned answers, not framers, so no fit: porter-tailnet `relay.rs` (a 503) and `lend.rs`, inferd `attached/check` tests, and porter-fake-servers `tailscale.rs` (chunked fixture bytes). porter-tailscale `client.rs` uses hyper.

**Tests.** The nine unit tests that moved keep their names under `stream::client::tests::*` and `stream::wire::tests::*`. Nothing was added, and the storage-webdav, storage-graph and storage-gdrive integration tests are unchanged apart from the imports they already had.

**Found on the way.** The sibling checkouts that sill, detent and casement path-depend on (quire, blitz-kit, shell-host, casement-protocols) were not at their origin/master, so their check worktrees link origin/master copies of quire, blitz-kit and shell-host instead. Resolving sill's graph with the sibling checkouts changes its origin/master `Cargo.lock` (the blitz rev, the `ds-desktop` package), and `--locked` refuses that, so the check uses `--offline`. Whether the base has the same drift was not checked. sill also takes porter-client, porter-provider, porter-secrets and porter-service from git through shell-host, so its check patches those four, as it already did the other porter crates.

## Lane load-4 (the resolve_bus conflict that never came, the last timing claims, a product sweep; base `a1a07f5`, 2026-10-10)

No cargo command was run (nobuild rules); the coordinator compiles and gates.

**1. resolve_bus (`79e839b`).** The failure ("a Conflict signal in time", 137 s) was a test race over a real product rule, not a lost signal.
- Read: `Driver::run` (`crates/syncd/src/driver.rs`) takes a pause between cycles only: a cycle already running finishes, and `Hub::set_pausing` (`service/hub.rs`) only flips a watch. The test paused, slept 400 ms and then made the remote edit and the local edit. On a loaded machine a cycle that began before the pause outlasted the 400 ms, fetched "theirs" before "mine" existed, and the later local edit uploaded over it as an ordinary change: no conflict was ever recorded, so the wait for the signal could only run out (120 s plus the load = 137 s). The cap was never the cause; widening or an idle deadline would only have waited longer for something that cannot happen.
- Checked and not at fault: the signal before the subscription (`receive_conflict` and `datasets()` come first, and the roster is joined); a resume that does not wake (`Nudge::Pause` wakes `wait`, `driver.rs` `wait`); `time_scale` (`Settings::quick` is 10, `step` divides by it, `sleep_until` re-reads the clock each step); `announce` tells every conflict of the report that created it, after the journal numbered it.
- Fix: `Hub::hold_cycles(&DatasetName)` (new, `hub.rs`): waits for the cycle in flight and holds the cycle lock while its guard lives. resolve_bus, storage_bus and gdrive_bus (the same 400 ms pattern) now pause, hold, make both edits, drop the hold, resume. The three waits for the signal are `porter_fake::GENEROUS` (was a literal 120 s). Row: `hub_tests::holding_the_cycles_waits_for_the_one_running_and_starts_no_other` (paused clock).
- pim_bus's torn-read test was already fixed on master (`Deadline::idle`); not touched.

**2. The three risks of fix-loadflake-3 (`2447520`).**
- accountd `scenario_fixes::all_changes` is gone. `changes_counting(changes, n)` waits GENEROUS for each of `n` changes, then a 1 s quiet window for extras (the exact-list assert is the caller's); the "says so once" test passes 1. The other two callers assert only `contains`, and I could not run the module to know how many changes a removal or a report sends, so they use `changes_until(changes, wanted)`, which reads until the wanted change comes. Not the count asked for; a count I could not check would have made a test that fails on a wrong guess.
- inferd `shutdown`: the 5 s and 4 s elapsed asserts compare with `LONG_MS` (60 s), the configured grace or bound. The graceful row's bound was 5000 ms and is now `LONG_MS`: with 5 s a slow stop on a starved machine exits `TIMED_OUT` and fails `DONE`. Meaning kept: the stop ended by its grace (`DONE`, not `TIMED_OUT`) and the second signal did not wait for the grace (`HURRIED`).
- porter-proxy `tls.rs`: the dial of a silent server is wrapped in `GENEROUS` (it must return by itself, `Unreachable`) and asserts `elapsed >= 150 ms` (the configured handshake timeout; a timer never fires early). The upper bound is gone.

**3. Sweep of syncd, accountd and inferd `tests/it`** (every `timeout(`, `from_secs`, `from_millis`, `sleep(`, `recv_timeout`, `elapsed`).
- Converted: the stalled-server rows (`5bf9040`, syncd `stalled.rs`): the slow-server row spaced pieces 120 ms against a 200 ms idle time on the wall clock (a loaded machine could make a gap longer than the idle time and fail a server that never went silent); both timed rows now run on the paused clock and the `< 10 s` assert counts virtual time. syncd `sync_bus` `sleep(100 ms)` after a pause (same race as item 1; `58b7850`). The three Conflict waits (item 1) and the items of item 2. All other waits for something to happen were already `GENEROUS`/`eventually` (fix-loadflake-3).
- Left, proofs that something does NOT happen (a late signal can only make them pass): `poll_time(..)` sleeps, `silent` (500 ms), the 300 ms drains (`photos_picker_bus`, `gdrive_bus`, `acceptance`, `common`), `told`/`hears_nothing`/`heard`/`quiet_for` windows, bus_sheets 200 ms, agent_launcher 200 ms and the `Witness` 500 ms, pim_bus 900-1200 ms, gphotos 2500 ms, tailnet 700 ms.
- Left, durations that ARE the behaviour or a setup that fails open: accountd `start`/inferd `start`/syncd `start` HELD (2 s; a late release lets the runtime start anyway, so the row can only get weaker), `bus_sheets` slow sheet 2 s, `engine_start` FAST 25 s / 30 s / 180 s (configured starts), `engine_group` 60 s linger, attached.rs 900 ms (the engine goes idle; longer only helps), `places` 150 ms signal retry (it loops to GENEROUS), `photos_picker_bus` 50 ms poll, `dist.rs` 600 s (a value asserted, not waited).

**4. Product sweep: wall-clock limits on a person's path** (src of syncd, accountd, inferd; excluding tests).
- Would fail on a loaded machine, fixed:
  - syncd `Driver::run`: a pause made while the driver waited for the cycle lock was ignored and one more cycle ran (`58b7850`). Not a number, a race: the pause is read again once the lock is held. Row: `driver_wake::a_pause_made_while_a_cycle_waits_for_its_turn_lets_no_cycle_through`.
  - inferd attached check `WAIT` 3 s per stage (`88d2fd7`, now 30 s): the look at an attached engine when a session opens reported "did not answer a model list" for an engine that was only slow. Row: `check::tests::an_engine_that_is_slow_to_answer_is_still_ready` (answers after 4 s, real time; passes at any load).
- Would not fail on a loaded machine, left as they are (value, why):
  - syncd: `RELAY_IDLE` 60 s (silence on the relay; per piece), `STOP_WITHIN` 300 s (a stop waiting for a running cycle), rescans 600 s (`pim` and `storage` supervisors, `paths.rs`), `next_look` backoff (min with the rescan), Google picker poll 5 s and timeout 600 s (from the service), `step` (poll interval, scaled).
  - accountd: `AUTHENTICATE_WITHIN` 60 s (relay dial and authenticate; matches the sign-in `WITHIN` of fix-loadflake-3), `CREATE_WINDOW` 60 s (a rate limit of 10 Spaces, a rule), tailnet `backstop` 30 s, `retry` 10 s, `coalesce` 1 s (cadences), launcher `DEFAULT_BOUND` 600 s and `DEFAULT_TICK` 1 s (a login's own life, on the wall clock).
  - inferd: `PROBE_TIMEOUT` 10 s in `daemon.rs` and `hosts.rs` (documented: three misses in a row before a runtime is marked offline, the supervisor asks again), `DOUBT_WAIT` 2 s, `RELOAD_EVERY` 2 s, `runner.rs` `TIMEOUTS` (connect 2 s on a local socket or loopback port is completed by the kernel and does not wait for the engine to run; first byte 120 s; idle 60 s), `cloud` (10/180/90 s), `RELAYED_WAIT` 60 s, `LOOK_WAIT` 15 s for listing a Tailscale computer (a candidate to raise if a starved machine shows it; a failed look is made again with backoff), `agent/handle.rs` 120 s and `lender.rs` `HEAD_WAIT` 120 s (refuse a client that sends nothing, by design), `STOP_GRACE` 5 s (the time an engine has to stop before it is killed, a behaviour), shutdown `BOUND` 15 s (under systemd's 20 s `TimeoutStopSec`, a behaviour: a stop that overruns exits non-zero and kills everything).

## Lane install-tailnet (the tailnet drop-in as data and the Settings switch's installer; base `a1a07f5`, 2026-10-10)

Owner's choice (c): `dist/inferd-tailnet.conf` stays opt-in. porter ships it as data; the Settings switch "Let my other computers use this computer's models" installs it, with the person's consent, through porter-client; detent only asks the question and calls the API. Why opt-in: the engines share inferd's sandbox and `IPAddressAllow` covers the whole unit, so an always-on drop-in would let engines reach the tailnet while `ai.tailnet.serve` is off.

**install.sh.** `put 644 dist/inferd-tailnet.conf $prefix/share/porter/inferd-tailnet.conf` (honours DESTDIR); the header, the drop-in's own comment and the Sandbox paragraph above say so. Test `the_tailnet_drop_in_is_installed_as_data_and_never_as_a_live_drop_in` (accountd `tests/it/install.rs`): same bytes, mode 0644, nothing under the home folder, no `*.service.d` directory, no `/etc/systemd`.

**API for detent and Whopper** (`porter-client`, feature `lending`; `SessionUnits` also needs `dbus`; `lending` brings only porter-fs, std only):
- `LendingConfig::new(shipped, systemd_user_dir)`, `::under_prefix(prefix, systemd_user_dir)`, `::from_env() -> Option<Self>` (HOME, XDG_CONFIG_HOME, XDG_DATA_HOME, XDG_DATA_DIRS; `None` with no home folder), `::from_lookup(var)` (the same over a closure), `.shipped()`, `.drop_in()`.
- `TailnetLending::new(config, manager)`; `state() -> LendingState`; `async enable() -> Result<(), LendingError>`; `async disable() -> Result<(), LendingError>`.
  - `enable` reads the shipped file, writes `inferd.service.d/tailnet.conf` atomically with mode 0644 (porter-fs), then `Reload`, then `RestartUnit("inferd.service", "replace")` and waits for that job's `JobRemoved`. Twice leaves the same file and still reloads and restarts, so a try that stopped after the write is finished.
  - `disable` removes the drop-in if present (and its folder if empty), then reloads and restarts. Idempotent. Neither touches `ai.tailnet.serve`; detent sets it (on after `enable` is `Ok`, off before or after `disable`).
- `LendingState` (`#[non_exhaustive]`): `NotInstalled`, `Installed` (byte-equal to the shipped file), `Differs` (present but different, or the shipped file cannot be read; the switch shows off and turning it on replaces it).
- `LendingError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, `Display` is plain words to show as they are): `ShippedFileMissing`, `CouldNotWrite(io::Error)`, `ReloadFailed(UnitFailure)`, `RestartFailed(UnitFailure)`. Causes are `source()`, for a log.
- `UnitManager` (trait: `reload()`, `restart(UnitName)`, both `Send` futures of `Result<(), UnitFailure>`), `UnitFailure(pub String)`, `UnitName`, `INFERD_UNIT`, `SHIPPED_NAME`.
- `SessionUnits::new(connection)` / `::session().await`: the real manager over the session bus (`org.freedesktop.systemd1` `Manager`; a restart that does not finish in 60 seconds fails).
- porter-dbus now exports `SYSTEMD`, `SYSTEMD_PATH`, `SYSTEMD_MANAGER` (they were private in `callers.rs`).
- Example: `cargo run -p porter-client --example lending --features lending,dbus [-- enable|disable]`, and the lib docs of porter-client.

**Tests** (`porter-client` `tests/it/tailnet_lending.rs`, no real systemd, `~/.config` or `/etc`): a fake manager that records calls and fails on request, over a scratch directory: exact bytes, mode (also over a 0600 file), call order, enable twice, Differs and the newer shipped file, disable (also with nothing there and with the person's other files beside), the failure rows (missing shipped file, a folder in the way, reload fails, restart fails, disable then restart fails), the plain-words table, the environment table; and `SessionUnits` on a private bus against a scripted manager (waits for its own job, ignores another job's end, a failed job, no manager).

**Boundary.** check-boundary.sh: `porter-client[dbus]` without `lending` or `in-process` reaches no porter-fs, and the `porter-client` edge list names porter-fs. Cargo.toml: `lending = ["dep:porter-fs"]`; Cargo.lock gains porter-fs under porter-client.

**Not compiled by the lane** (no-build rules): the coordinator's gate is the first compile of this lane.

If a doc test fails, look first at porter-infer (the `ChatRequest` field list) and porter-client (`no_run` still compiles it).

## Lane layers-router (inferd's routing, local model book and session machine as a library; base `a1a07f5`, 2026-10-10)

**Not compiled.** Written under the no-build rule: the coordinator compiles and gates. Every move is `git mv` (history follows) plus path fixes; `inferd` re-exports every moved path with `pub use`, so no `inferd::...` path a consumer or a test names has changed.

**The set, and why each module is in or out** (inferd's `src` module tree at `a1a07f5`):
- IN `router` (+ `router::placed`): pure over a list of models. `check_class` (was `cua_run::check_class`) and `slots_of` (was `settings::keys::slots_of`) moved in with it, since the route and the plan need them.
- IN `swap`: pure over stoker's `budget`.
- IN `catalog` and `local` (the model book): `LocalModel`, `EngineConfig`, `build`. `NamedEngine` (the `[engines.<name>]` replay table, was in `replay`) moved to `local` because `EngineConfig` embeds it; `replay` keeps the path.
- IN `attached::{config, key, target, model}` and `NotReady` (split out of `attached::check`): the attached engines as values, because `LocalModel.attached` and `EngineConfig.attached` need them. OUT: `attached::{check (the probe), book, computers}`: they connect and write files, they are the daemon's.
- IN `session` (the machine and its tables), `seams` (was `serve/seams.rs`), `carried` (was `serve/carried.rs`), a new `audio` (`check_audio`, `audio_ms`, `AudioIn`, `AudioPull`, cut out of `speech`) and `startup` (`Cause`, `Tail`, `Log`: `EngineFailed` carries a `Cause`). OUT: `serve::{driver, wire}` (the fd server: rustix, tokio, Unix sockets).
- IN `pipeline` planning half (`catalogue_of`, `granted_of`, `plan`, `decide_text`): the route of a language session goes through it. The stage runner is porter-turns; `Hearing` (needs `Engines`, `Turns`) stays in inferd.
- OUT `engines` (`Engines`, `SessionRouter`): it is the glue of `Settings`/`Live`, `Cloud`, `peers`, `probed`, `supervise`. It is inferd's, and it is the one that implements the `Router` seam over the library's `choose`. OUT `auto` (the `[ai.auto]` settings rows), `settings`, `supervise`, `hosts`, `cloud`, `agent`, `probe`, `probed`, `replay`, `tailnet`, `peers`, `service`, `daemon`, `watch`, `report`, `speech` (the speech host runners), `config`, `clock`, `shutdown`.

**API.** The crate lib doc lists the pieces; root re-exports of the main names (`Listed`, `Decided`, `choose`, `LocalModel`, `EngineConfig`, `build`, `Seams`, the five seams, `SessionSpec`, `step`). Added: constructors `Seams::new`, `EngineFailed::new`, `Budget::new`; `unix_endpoint`, `loopback_endpoint` (were in `hosts`, which re-exports them) and `LocalModel::endpoint`, where a model's engine is reached; a compiled doctest (`check_class`). No function reads the environment (check-boundary greps for it). Feature `testing` exposes `testkit` (`Scratch`, `models`, `cloud_card`, `entries`), which inferd's and porter-turns' tests use as a dev-dependency.
- Deliberately not `#[non_exhaustive]`: the types inferd builds with a struct literal (`Seams`, `Budget`, `Listed`, `LocalModel`, `EngineConfig`, `Carried`, `SessionSpec`, `Decided`...), and the enums its code matches exhaustively (`Cause`, `NotReady`, `Phase`...). Sealing them is lane L5's (constructors first, then the attribute), and inferd's `Engines` would change with it. Constructors are added where one was missing and is useful without sealing.

**Visibility widened** (all `pub(crate)` or `pub(super)` that a caller in another crate now needs): `router::fits` (inferd's `engines` and `engines::places` call it), `carried::Tally` and its `begin`, `heard`, `closing` (the fd server's loop, in inferd). Kept `pub(crate)`: `router::consent_of`, `router::plain`, `local::flavor_of`, `session::model_of` (all used only inside the crate).

**Boundary.** check-boundary: `porter-router` forbids EFFECTS (zbus, tokio, hyper, ...) and `inferd`; an EDGES row (porter-core, porter-infer, cua-action, engine-supervisor, model-catalog, model-http (types only: no `hyper`), model-openai-compat, model-provider, speech-provider, vision-prep); a grep rule that the inference libraries read no environment variable; `check-portable.sh` checks the crate builds and reaches no zbus. inferd's EDGES row gains `porter-router`.

**Tests.** They moved with their code (swap, catalog, local, router, placed, session and its `step_tests` tables, carried, startup, attached key/model/config, audio). Two tests of the attached tables read `inferd.toml` (`InferdConfig`) and stayed in inferd as `attached/config_file_tests.rs`. The `pipeline` tests (a run over the replay engine and `Supervised`) stay in inferd: they are edge tests of the plan and the run together. `testkit::{Scratch, models, cloud_card}` and the test catalog entries (`entries.rs`, with the `qwen3.5-35b-a3b-fp8.toml` fixture) moved to the router's `testkit`; inferd's `testkit` re-exports them and keeps `Recorder` (it needs tokio and stoker's `EngineHost`). The integration tests (`tests/it/hosting/mod.rs`) take `entries` from there.

**Look here first if it does not compile.**
- `crates/porter-router/Cargo.toml`: dependency set read off the `use` lines (needs a re-lock: new crate).
- `crates/inferd/src/attached.rs`: module aliases (`pub use porter_router::attached::{config, key, model, target}`) so `super::config::...` in `book`, `check` and `computers` still resolves.
- `crates/inferd/src/lib.rs`: `#[cfg(test)] use porter_router::testkit::entries;` stands in for the old `#[path]` module, so `crate::entries` in the unit tests resolves.
- `crates/porter-router/src/session.rs`: the `#[path = "session/step_tests/mod.rs"]` module moved with it.
- `crates/inferd/src/pipeline/tests.rs`: explicit imports replace what `use super::*` took from the old `pipeline.rs`.

**Open.** (1) `speech::SpeechRunner` and `Ears` implement the audio seams in inferd; an app that wants a speech turn without inferd has the seam (`AudioIn`) and no runner. (2) The `Router` seam's implementation, `engines::SessionRouter`, stays in inferd; a third party writes its own `Listed` list and calls `choose`.

## Lane layers-turns (inferd's turn running, structured replies and audit as a library; base `a1a07f5`, after layers-router, 2026-10-10)

**Not compiled.** As above.

**The set, and why each is in or out:**
- IN `bridge` (+ `bridge::request`), `tee`, `structured` (+ `limits`), `cua_step`, `cua_run`, `audit`, and the stage runner of `pipeline` (was `pipeline/run.rs`): they depend on the router crate's `LocalModel`, seams and clock, on stoker, and on nothing of inferd's. `structured::limits` keeps `Limits`, `StructuredConfig` (the `[ai.structured]` rows) and `Resolved`; `AiConfig`, the whole `[ai]` table of `inferd.toml`, stays in inferd (`structured/ai.rs`) because it holds every settings row, and `AiConfig::resolve` calls `StructuredConfig::resolve`.
- `runner` is split, not moved. IN (new `porter_turns::local`): `LocalTurns<U: EngineUse>`: the chat, embedding and computer-use turns of a session pinned to a `LocalModel` (was `Job::{chat, checked, embed, cua}`), `TokioSleep`, `RETRY`, `chat_provider`, `ToSession`, `note_no_scores`, and the new `run_chat`. New `turn::Turn` (the turn in flight, was in `runner`). OUT, in inferd's `runner`: `Pin`/`Pinned` (they hold a `CloudPin`), `Turns` (adds the hosted model's turn, the speech turn, `Hearing`), and the `TurnRunner` impl. Why: a hosted model's turn needs `Cloud` (accountd's verdicts and keys, spend) and a speech turn needs the speech host; neither belongs in a library an app uses without the daemon.
- `EngineUse` is the one new trait: "this engine is in use", which `Supervised` implements in inferd (`supervise.rs`) by calling its own `used`; `()` implements it for an app that starts no engine.
- OUT `pipeline::live` (`Hearing` needs `Engines`), `speech`, `cloud`, `agent`.

**Visibility widened:** `Turn::running` (was `pub(crate)`, now `pub`), `note_no_scores` (was `pub(crate)`), `RETRY` (was `pub(crate)`). New `pub` only: `Turn::listening`, `ToSession::new`, `LocalTurns`' methods, `EngineUse`, `run_chat`, `Limits::new`.

**Behaviour.** Unchanged for inferd. `Forward` became generic over its two closures (`events`, `touch`) so inferd's plain chat turn and porter-client's share it (`run_chat`); inferd's `touch` is the same every-250-ms call of `Supervised::used`. `LocalModel::endpoint` (router) replaces the three-way endpoint choice that was in `runner::client`.

**What a third-party app now depends on to route and run turns:** `porter-router` (the list of models it has -> `router::choose` / `pipeline::decide_text` -> a `Decided`; `catalog` + `local::build` to list this computer's models from stoker's catalogue; `session::step` and the `seams` if it runs a session server) and `porter-turns` (`local::LocalTurns` or `local::run_chat` over any `model_provider::Provider`, `structured`, `audit`, `pipeline::run_pipeline`), plus `porter-infer` and `porter-core` for the vocabulary and stoker's crates by git rev. No zbus, no inferd, no accountd. It needs a tokio runtime for the turns (none for the router).

**porter-client `engines` (item 3).** Rebuilt over `porter-turns` (`dep:porter-turns` in the feature): removed `TokioSleep` and the `RETRY` const (now `porter_turns::local::{TokioSleep, RETRY}`) from `engines/wire.rs`, and the `Forward` sink with its `provider.turn` / `chat_reply` / `model_error` match from `engines/turn.rs` (now `run_chat`): 48 lines deleted, 17 added. The client's reply is the same (its events go to its own channel, it has no engine to touch and, as before, says nothing of option shares). Not changed, because it is not the same logic: the table and `admit` (the client's rule is "nothing is chosen by default", the router's `choose` picks by tier and readiness), `EngineSession` (a one-turn-at-a-time session that refuses a second request; the session machine queues one), the `Temperature` body shaping and the TLS engine (inferd's `cloud::wire` has the same two-line shaping for a hosted model: a later move into porter-turns would remove that copy too). porter-router is not a client dependency: nothing in the client duplicates what it holds. The cost: the `engines` feature now also builds `porter-turns` and what it brings (porter-router, stoker's `cua-session`, `cua-vendors`, `cua-parse`, `model-extract`, `engine-supervisor`, `vision-prep` with `pixels`).

**Line counts (inferd, `.rs`, tests inside `src` included):** `src` 35,664 before; 28,359 after layers-router; 24,949 after layers-turns. `tests/` 13,803 before, 13,582 after (`entries.rs` moved). The new crates: porter-router 7,772 (of which about 3,550 are unit tests), porter-turns 3,628 (about 1,930 unit tests).

**Boundary.** check-boundary: `porter-turns` forbids zbus, zvariant, reqwest, ureq, oo7, keyring, secret-service, interprocess, latchkey, ds-settings and `inferd` (it may reach tokio and hyper: it runs turns); an EDGES row; the environment grep covers it; `porter-client`'s EDGES row gains `porter-turns` (feature `engines`); `check-portable.sh` checks it builds and reaches no zbus.

**Tests.** Moved with their code: `bridge::request`, `structured`, `cua_step`, `cua_run`, `audit`. `[ai.structured]`'s tests (`limits_tests`) read `inferd.toml`, so they stayed in inferd (`structured/limits_tests.rs`). `runner`'s tests stayed with the part that stayed (they now import `RunningTurn` themselves). The `pipeline` tests stayed (see layers-router).

**Look here first if it does not compile.** `crates/porter-turns/src/local.rs` (`run_chat`'s `Send` bounds on the two closures; `TurnSink: Send`), `crates/inferd/src/runner.rs` (`Job::local()` builds a `LocalTurns<Supervised>`; `Supervised: Sync` for the borrow across the await), `crates/inferd/src/structured.rs` and `structured/ai.rs` (the `limits` module re-exports), `crates/porter-client/src/engines/turn.rs` (the `send` closure's parameter type is inferred from `UnboundedSender::send`), the new manifests (re-lock: `porter-router`, `porter-turns`, and `porter-turns` under porter-client).

**Open.** (1) `runner::Turns` could become `porter_turns::Turns<H>` over a trait for the hosted and speech turns once `Cloud` has a seam; not done, it is a redesign. (2) `cloud::wire`'s body shaping and `porter-client`'s `Shaped` are the same code twice; both could use one in porter-turns. (3) L5 (constructors and `#[non_exhaustive]`) for the types listed under layers-router.

**Not compiled.** No-build lane: only `cargo fmt --all` was run. Cargo.lock is the coordinator's (every stoker git source changes rev; `model-provider` gains no new dependency this lane knows of, check the lock diff).

- `Constraint::JsonObject` and `OutputShape::JsonObject` (`model-extract` picks it after `JsonSchema` and `Gbnf`, only for an object-shaped reply, puts the schema in the system prompt and still validates). `enforceable(flavor)` lists it for every flavor.
- `cua_parse::SCROLL_PIXELS_RULE`; catalogue entries `holo-3.1-9b`, `holo4-35b-a3b-fp8` (both `json_schema`), `deepseek-v4-pro` (`json_object`), `muse-spark-1.3-contributor`.
- `speech-vad-silero`: `SileroConfig.runtime: OnnxRuntimePath`, `OrtInfer::load(dylib, model)`, no env read. **Porter does not depend on this crate** (grep for silero, speech-vad and ORT_DYLIB_PATH finds nothing in code), so there is nothing to pass and no `Config::from_env` change; the person's `ORT_DYLIB_PATH` is unaffected. (inferd's speech host takes its libraries from `speech_host_libs`, a different path.)
- Internal only, no porter change: `cua-parse` tools take a `ToolDialect` instead of a `holo` bool, Holo's `finish` reads `text` when there is no `summary`, `cua-vendors` `AnthropicTool`, tests moved to `tests/it`.

**Changes.**
- `ModelError` (porter-infer, serde `kind`) gains `PaymentRequired` ("the account needs payment") and `SignInRefused` ("the company refused the sign-in"), wire slugs `payment_required` and `sign_in_refused`. The provider's own message stays out of the error (it is in the logs of stoker's side only). **Consumer break:** docket's exhaustive match on `ModelError` needs the two arms (as for `OnlyThought` before). I did not mark the enum `#[non_exhaustive]` because inferd's exhaustive `Failure::of_model` would then need a wildcard; say if you want it.
- `porter_bridge::reply::model_error`: `PaymentRequired(_)` to `PaymentRequired`, `AuthRejected(_)` to `SignInRefused`. Row each in `every_provider_error_is_a_model_error_the_app_can_act_on`; slug rows in `porter-infer` `frames.rs`.
- inferd `agent::fail::Failure::of_model`: both are `Cause::Upstream` with the same plain sentences (like `Unauthorized`).
- porter-router `catalog::llm_cap`: `StructuredOutput` is claimed for `JsonSchema` or `JsonObject`, since a JSON-object model answers a shape through `model-extract`.
- porter-turns `structured` tests: `ran` split into `ran` and `ran_on(model, ...)`; new test `a_model_that_takes_only_a_json_object_gets_the_schema_in_the_prompt_and_is_still_checked` (first reply invalid, repaired; request output is `JsonObject`, schema text in a leading system message).
- porter-router `catalog` tests: the shipped list also names `holo-3.1-9b` and `holo4-35b-a3b-fp8`.

**Exhaustive matches touched:** `porter_bridge::reply::model_error` (ProviderError), `inferd::agent::fail::Failure::of_model` (ModelError). No exhaustive match on `Constraint` or `OutputShape` exists in porter (only `contains`, and `scores.rs` matches `Choice` with a fallthrough).

**Open.** The unchecked path (`porter_bridge::request::chat_turn_for`, used when a request has tools, the schema is not one the vocabulary can say, or the model has no caps) still sends `OutputShape::JsonSchema` for a `ReplyShape::Json`; `Target` carries no capabilities, so it cannot know a model takes only a JSON object. A `deepseek-v4-pro`-like model with tools plus a JSON shape would be sent a schema it cannot take. Fixing it means a `Target` field for the output constraints.

**Look here first if it does not compile.** `crates/porter-turns/src/structured/tests.rs` (`caps.output` is a `BTreeSet`, collected from `std::iter::once`; `LocalModel` comes from `use super::*`), `crates/porter-bridge/src/reply/tests.rs` (`sp::ProviderDetail::new(status: u16, &str)`).
